.. cpp:struct:: EffectInstanceCounters final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_registry/effect_registry.hpp>``

   What an instance has counted over its life.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t ticks

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t skipped_ticks

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t frames

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t setups

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t refused_calls

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t dropped_input

      Input events dropped: past maximum_effect_input_events before a frame.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: EffectLoadDiagnostics final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_registry/effect_registry.hpp>``

   What effect registration found in this process, for diagnostics and the windows' metrics (MetricsSnapshot::effects_unverified): effects registered, and those registered unverified. The first unverified registration also writes one line to stderr naming it and saying why; the rest are only counted. Any thread.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t registered

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t unverified

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t refused

      Selections the load checks refused (note_effect_refused).

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: EffectPointer final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect/effect.hpp>``

   The pointer's standing state over the part at the frame's start (design note section 8a(1)): whether it is inside the part, whether a button is held, and where it was last seen while inside, in part-local logical units. Filled only for an effect that declares pointer input on a part whose host grants it; otherwise every field is zero, as in headless runs and still poses, so motion checksums do not depend on it.

   .. cpp:member:: bool inside

   .. cpp:member:: bool pressed

   .. cpp:member:: Point position

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: EffectRandom final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect/effect.hpp>``

   The engine's deterministic generator (owner amendment, 2026-11-02): a counter-based hash of (seed, stream, counter), SplitMix64's finalizer over their mix, with no host randomness and no hidden state beyond the counter in this value. Bit-exact on every host; known-answer vectors are in the tests.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr EffectRandom(std::uint64_t seed, std::uint64_t stream) noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static constexpr std::uint64_t hash(std::uint64_t seed, std::uint64_t stream, std::uint64_t counter) noexcept

      The hash itself: the same three inputs give the same bits.

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr std::uint64_t next() noexcept

      The next 64 bits.

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr double next_unit() noexcept

      In [0, 1): the top 53 bits, exactly representable.

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr std::uint32_t next_below(std::uint32_t bound) noexcept

      In [0, bound): the high half of a 32 × 32-bit product; 0 when bound is 0.

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr double next_between(double low, double high) noexcept

      In [low, high).

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::uint64_t setup_stream

      The stream setup draws from. Default: ``0xffffffffffffffffULL``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: EffectRegistry final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_registry/effect_registry.hpp>``


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t count() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t unverified_count() const noexcept

      How many registered effects carry no verification record.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: EffectRegistrationOutcome add(const EffectEntry& entry) noexcept

      Checks ``entry`` and adds it; anything but ``registered`` leaves the registry unchanged. Looks up the plugin's verification record for it.

   .. cpp:function:: const EffectEntry* find(std::string_view name) const noexcept

      The entry registered under ``name``, or null.

   .. cpp:function:: const EffectEntry& at(std::size_t index) const noexcept

.. cpp:struct:: EffectSelection final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_registry/effect_registry.hpp>``

   What a control's effect selection came to, kept by the control for diagnostics: the name asked for (a copy, empty for none), who asked and the verdict.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string name

   .. cpp:member:: EffectSelectionSource source

      Default: ``{EffectSelectionSource::none}``.

   .. cpp:member:: EffectSelectionOutcome outcome

      Default: ``{EffectSelectionOutcome::none}``.

.. cpp:struct:: EffectSetup final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect/effect.hpp>``

   What ``setup`` is given: once when the effect is instantiated on a part, and again on a geometry change if the effect declares setup_on_geometry. Its draws come from EffectRandom(seed, EffectRandom::setup_stream).

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t seed

   .. cpp:member:: Size part

   .. cpp:member:: double scale

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: EngineExpression final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/engine_expression/engine_expression.hpp>``

   A compiled program: immutable, a fixed-size value with no allocation, so it can be copied into a rule table and evaluated from any number of parts on the UI thread.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: EngineExpression() noexcept

      The program ``1`` with fallback 0, which reads nothing.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: std::span<const ExpressionInstruction> instructions() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t operation_count() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: double fallback() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: const ExpressionDependencies& dependencies() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: const ExpressionLimits& limits() const noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: ExpressionCompilation compile(std::span<const ExpressionInstruction> program, double fallback, ExpressionLimits limits) noexcept

      Checks ``program`` against :cpp:func:`limits <gui_forms::EngineExpression::limits>` and the budget, then replaces this expression with it. Anything but ``none`` leaves the expression unchanged. :cpp:func:`fallback <gui_forms::EngineExpression::fallback>` is the value an evaluation gives on a division by zero or a value that is not finite.

   .. cpp:function:: ExpressionResult evaluate(const ExpressionEnvironment& environment) const noexcept

      Evaluates the program. Its cost is one operation per instruction (operation_count). Never fails: a defect of the inputs gives the fallback, with the reason.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: EnginePartRecord final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

   One painted part's record. The identity strings are owned copies of the key the part was first painted with.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string control

   .. cpp:member:: std::string part

   .. cpp:member:: std::string item

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<std::size_t> item_index

      Set only for the index fallback (PartKey::index_fallback).

   .. cpp:member:: AnatomyStateSet states

      The states the part was last painted in.

   .. cpp:member:: PrimitivePreparation preparation

      What the part's primitive derived for its geometry; empty until a primitive draws the part (never, before themes select one in T3).

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t last_painted

      When the part was last painted: the number of window paints completed before the one that drew it. The cache evicts the smallest first.

   .. cpp:member:: bool motion_unsettled

      True while a transition or motion value of the part has not settled (E3, E7): such a record is never evicted. Nothing sets it before E3.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t inputs_read

      What the part's last paint read, and where it was drawn (E6). An input change damages exactly this extent of this control. - ``inputs_read``: the inputs its primitive and its expressions read, as expression input bits (expression_input_bit); zero for a part the built-in recipe draws. Set only through EnginePartTable::note_paint, which keeps the table's reader counts. - ``painted_by``: the stable ID of the control whose display chunk recorded the part. It is the control painting the part, or one that paints on its behalf, such as a docking site's drag layer. - ``extent``: the part's bounds and its primitive's outsets, in that control's local coordinates.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string painted_by

   .. cpp:member:: Rect extent

   .. cpp:member:: bool reads_backplane

      Whether the part's last paint sampled the window's backplane (E9-B): backplane damage within its extent repaints it.

   .. cpp:member:: FrameTime state_entered

      When the part entered the states it was last painted in, on the window's motion clock; time_in_state counts from it.

   .. cpp:member:: std::unique_ptr<PartMotion> motion

      The part's transitions (E7), made the first time it is painted with one; null for a part without any.

   .. cpp:member:: std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster> shown_face

      For a part with a face cross-fade (E7r form a): the face it showed last and the states it showed it in, and while a fade runs the face it fades from. A fade starts only when the states change (a discrete change), never when a keyframe step or a scale replaces the face. The faces are shared with the raster cache and the display; they keep a replaced face alive only until its fade settles, and are released on the first paint without a face transition.

   .. cpp:member:: std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster> fading_face

   .. cpp:member:: AnatomyStateSet face_states

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<AnatomyStateSet> keyframe_states

      The states whose keyframes were last queued for idle frames (E7r): queued again when the part's states change.

   .. cpp:member:: bool ambient

      Whether the part's last paint had an ambient effect, and whether that effect was refused for its area (E7). An ambient part repaints when ambient motion resumes; a refusal is counted once, when it begins.

   .. cpp:member:: bool ambient_refused

   .. cpp:member:: bool primitive_refused

      Whether the part's primitive refused its last paint (E8c, EnginePrimitive::accepts), so that it was drawn by the built-in recipe; a refusal is counted once, when it begins.

   .. cpp:member:: std::unique_ptr<CachedPartRaster> cached

      The cached raster and what drew it, made the first time a primitive draws the part, so that a record no primitive draws stays small.

   .. cpp:member:: std::unique_ptr<AssembledPartMaterial> assembled_material

      The part's assembled material, made the first time a material primitive draws the part with a material field a rule sets (EC1).

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t generation

      The record's current raster generation; invalidate_raster advances it. Default: ``{1U}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: EnginePartTable final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

   The table. It owns its records. Every member runs on the window's UI thread; the table is not shared between windows.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: explicit EnginePartTable(std::size_t item_record_cap = maximum_item_part_records) noexcept

      The table keeps at most ``item_record_cap`` item-part records (at least 1); the window's table uses maximum_item_part_records.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t record_count() const noexcept

      Live records, and those of item parts among them.

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t item_record_count() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t slot_count() const noexcept

      Record storage allocated, live or free: released and evicted slots are reused before it grows, so it stays near the most records live at once.

   .. cpp:function:: bool idle_keyframes_waiting() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: bool patterns_waiting() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: bool finish_tiles_waiting() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: RasterCacheStatistics cache_statistics() const noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: PartRecordAcquisition acquire(std::string_view control_id, const PartKey& key)

      The record for ``control_id`` and ``key``, made if there is none, and marked the most recently painted item record when it is an item's. A new record reuses a released slot before the table grows; a new item record beyond the cap evicts the least recently painted settled item record (O(1) unless records with unsettled motion are passed over). If an allocation fails, std::bad_alloc propagates and the table is unchanged.

   .. cpp:function:: std::optional<PartRecordHandle> find(std::string_view control_id, const PartKey& key) const

      The live record for ``control_id`` and ``key``, if there is one. For inspection: it builds its lookup text in a string of its own.

   .. cpp:function:: EnginePartRecord* record(PartRecordHandle handle) noexcept

      The record a handle names, or null when the handle is stale (its record was released) or names no slot. The pointer is valid until the next acquire() or release on this table.

   .. cpp:function:: const EnginePartRecord* record(PartRecordHandle handle) const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t release_control(std::string_view control_id) noexcept

      Releases every record of the control with this stable ID, as when it leaves the window, and returns how many. Their handles become stale and their cached rasters are released. The cost is one pass over the table's slots.

   .. cpp:function:: PartRasterResult raster_for(PartRecordHandle handle, const PartRasterRequest& request, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      The part's raster for ``request``, painted on window paint ``paint_sequence`` (the record's new last_painted). - The cached raster is returned unchanged while its primitive, parameters, target, read states and extent are the request's and neither generation has advanced. - Otherwise the primitive draws the part. It draws into the cached raster's storage when the extent and scale are unchanged and no recorded command still holds that raster (a held raster is never rewritten, since a recording shows it), and into new storage otherwise. - New storage is made room for by evicting other parts' rasters, least recently painted first, until the cache fits its budget. A raster larger than the whole budget is drawn and returned but not kept. - The record's preparation is remade when the primitive refuses it as stale. If the primitive refuses, nothing is evicted and the record keeps its cached raster. Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc, with the record unchanged.

   .. cpp:function:: void queue_idle_keyframe(const PartRasterRequest& request)

      Idle keyframes (E7r; owner A, 2026-10-22). queue_idle_keyframe keeps a copy of ``request`` (its lent material copied, its borrows cleared) to bake in an idle frame, unless the table holds or queues it already or the queue is full. bake_idle_keyframe bakes the oldest queued keyframe the table does not hold, lending it ``images`` (a queued material whose images changed is dropped), and returns whether any remain queued. A theme change (invalidate_rasters) empties the queue. Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc, the queue then holding what it held less the keyframe taken.

   .. cpp:function:: bool bake_idle_keyframe(const ImageRegistry* images, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

   .. cpp:function:: PatternGeneration generate_pattern(std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      Procedural patterns (E10). A bake that asks for a pattern not held queues it (at most maximum_queued_patterns) and draws the fill's ground; nothing is generated inside a paint. generate_pattern generates the oldest queued pattern not held and holds it. Making one ends the currency of every raster that drew a ground (CachedPartRaster::awaited_patterns): the shared bakes among them and the backplane images are dropped, and the window repaints the parts (awaits_pattern), or every part when a raster without a record drew one, since it records no place. A pattern that cannot be made is dropped from the queue. Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc.

   .. cpp:function:: bool awaits_pattern(const CachedPartRaster& cached) const noexcept

      Whether ``cached`` drew a ground for a pattern made since.

   .. cpp:function:: std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster> finish_tile(const FinishTileKey& key, PartRecordHandle waiter, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      World finish tiles (cp31). finish_tile returns the tile held for ``key``, marked used by ``paint_sequence``, or null after queueing it (unless queued already or the queue is full) and noting ``waiter``, the part that asked, to be repainted. Nothing is painted inside a paint. generate_finish_tiles paints queued tiles, oldest first, until an idle frame's effort (finish_tile_idle_effort) is spent, at least one; it returns true when it held a tile and the queue is then empty: the waiters are to be repainted (take_finish_tile_waiters), once for the whole queue rather than once a tile. Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc and leaves the tile being painted unheld.

   .. cpp:function:: bool generate_finish_tiles(std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

   .. cpp:function:: void take_finish_tile_waiters(std::vector<PartRecordHandle>& waiters)

      Moves the parts waiting for finish tiles into ``waiters`` (cleared first) and forgets them.

   .. cpp:function:: PartRasterResult raster_without_record(const PartRasterRequest& request, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      A raster for a part painted without a key, which has no record: drawn for this paint and never kept (status uncached), assembled from a slice reference when the primitive slices it, which is kept. Refused as raster_for is; allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc.

   .. cpp:function:: void invalidate_raster(PartRecordHandle handle) noexcept

      Makes one part's cached raster stale; the next raster_for draws it again, and bakes again the slice reference it is assembled from. A stale handle does nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: void invalidate_rasters() noexcept

      Makes every cached raster stale and drops every slice reference, as when the theme changes.

   .. cpp:function:: void note_paint(PartRecordHandle handle, std::uint64_t inputs_read, std::string_view painted_by, Rect extent)

      Records what a part's paint read and where it was drawn (E6; see EnginePartRecord::inputs_read). A stale handle does nothing. If the control's ID cannot be copied, std::bad_alloc propagates and the record is unchanged.

   .. cpp:function:: void note_backplane_reader(PartRecordHandle handle, bool reads) noexcept

      Records whether a part's last paint sampled the window's backplane (E9-B), so that backplane damage under it repaints it. A stale handle does nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster> held_backplane(const BackplaneRegion& region, std::uint64_t generation, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      The backplane image held for ``region`` at the backplane's damage ``generation``, or null; one that is held is marked used by ``paint_sequence`` and counted a hit. Images of an earlier generation are dropped first.

   .. cpp:function:: void hold_backplane(const BackplaneRegion& region, std::uint64_t generation, const std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster>& image, std::uint64_t skipped, bool replayed, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      Holds ``image``, just made for ``region`` at ``generation``, replayed whole (``replayed``) or with ``skipped`` backplane commands left out, within the shared budget (least recently used evicted first; an image larger than the budget is counted but not held). Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc and holds nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: void bake_backplane_material(const SurfaceMaterial& material, Size window_size, const BackplaneRegion& region, const ImageRegistry* images, const PartRasterView& image, std::uint64_t paint_sequence)

      Draws the window's backplane ``material`` over ``region`` into ``image`` (E9-B), for a window of ``window_size`` logical units whose images are ``images`` (null for none): the material kernel over that region of the window's box, with the material's image variants lent as for a part. Nothing is drawn when the material is invalid.

   .. cpp:function:: bool reads_any(std::uint64_t inputs) const noexcept

      Whether any live record reads any input of ``inputs``, from the table's reader counts, without a scan.

   .. cpp:function:: const EnginePartRecord* record_in_slot(std::size_t slot) const noexcept

      The live record in ``slot`` (below slot_count()), or null for a free slot: for scans over the table. Valid until the next acquire() or release.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: EnginePrimitive

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/primitive_registry/primitive_registry.hpp>``

   One kind of effect. A primitive is immutable and holds no per-part state: one instance serves every part, on the UI thread. Per-part derived values live in the caller's PrimitivePreparation.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: PreparationOutcome prepare(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, const PrimitiveTarget& target, PrimitivePreparation& preparation) const noexcept

      Validates the target and the parameters, then derives the preparation's values. Anything but ``prepared`` leaves ``preparation`` unchanged. Its cost is the descriptor's preparation_per_device_pixel for each device pixel of the part's box.

   .. cpp:function:: RasterOutcome rasterize(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, const PrimitiveTarget& target, const PrimitivePreparation& preparation, const PartRasterView& destination) const noexcept

      Validates the destination, the target, the parameters and the preparation once, then fills every pixel of the destination with premultiplied BGRA8. Anything but ``painted`` leaves the destination's bytes unchanged.


   .. rubric:: Extension points

   .. cpp:function:: virtual const PrimitiveDescriptor& descriptor() const noexcept = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual bool blank(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters) const noexcept

      Whether the primitive draws nothing at all with ``parameters``, such as a bulge at amplitude 0 (the flat plane): the engine then composites nothing and keeps no raster for the part (E7). False unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual PartShape shape(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters) const noexcept

      The shape the primitive draws with ``parameters``, its radius in logical units (E8): what the part's hit region derives from (design language §7.4 rule 1). A rectangle filling the box unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual Insets content_insets(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, Size logical, DeviceScale scale, double text_scale) const noexcept

      How far inside a part of ``logical`` size, at display ``scale`` and the window's ``text_scale``, its text may begin (E8, T1's hand-off: text is published as a layout slot, ThemeEngine::text_slot). In logical units; the slot inside them lies within the shape the primitive draws. None unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations; ``logical`` and ``scale`` are positive and finite.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual std::optional<ControlVisualRole> theme_material(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, ControlVisualRole family) const noexcept

      The recipe role (ControlVisualRole) whose theme material the primitive draws with ``parameters`` (E8-1) on a part of recipe family ``family``: the engine then resolves the role's recipe for the part's state and lends its material in the target, with the material fields the theme's rules set in place of the recipe's (EC1). None unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual bool lends_overlay_shadow() const noexcept

      Whether the primitive draws the theme's overlay shadow (E9, GIVEN E1-1): the engine then lends the theme's overlay shadow layers for the part's presentation as a material of one transparent fill and those shadows, at the part's corner radius, and draws nothing when the theme casts none. False unless the primitive says otherwise.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual std::optional<PointerOverlay> pointer_overlay(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, Size part) const noexcept

      How the primitive is composited over a part of ``part`` size when it stands behind the overlay seam (PointerOverlay); none for a primitive drawn over the part's own box, unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual std::optional<std::uint32_t> backplane_margin(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, DeviceScale scale) const noexcept

      How many device pixels past the destination, on every side, the primitive reads the backplane at display ``scale`` (PrimitiveTarget:: backplane); none for a primitive that does not sample it, unless the primitive says otherwise. ``parameters`` match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual std::size_t image_variants(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, const PrimitiveTarget& target, std::span<ImageVariantRequest> requests) const noexcept

      Fills the front of ``requests`` with the image variants a bake of this target needs, at most one per fill layer, and returns how many; the engine lends them in PrimitiveTarget::variants. None unless the primitive says otherwise. Called as accepts() is, with a target the primitive accepts; ``requests`` holds maximum_image_variants entries.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual bool accepts(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, const PrimitiveTarget& target) const noexcept

      Whether the primitive will draw this target with ``parameters``, such as a material whose bake would exceed its bound. A primitive that refuses is drawn by the built-in recipe instead, and the engine counts the refusal. True unless the primitive says otherwise. Called only with a target in range and parameters that match the declarations.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual PrimitiveSlicePlan slice_plan(const PrimitiveParameterBlock& parameters, const PrimitiveTarget& target, DeviceExtent extent) const noexcept

      How a raster of ``extent`` for this target may be assembled from a shorter reference bake (E7r slicing). A plan that slices an axis promises that assembling the reference gives the bytes a whole bake would. Never slices unless the primitive says otherwise. Called as accepts() is, with a target the primitive accepts.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: EngineSineCosine final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/engine_math/engine_math.hpp>``

   The sine and cosine of ``x`` radians, computed together (E3's springs). For x of magnitude at most 2^20 the absolute error of each is at most 2^-50; beyond that, and for infinities and NaN, both are NaN. Exact case: x = 0 gives sine 0 and cosine 1. The argument is reduced by the nearest multiple of π/2, with π/2 split in three parts (Cody and Waite) so that the reduction is exact to about 2^-100 relative.

   .. cpp:member:: double sine

   .. cpp:member:: double cosine

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: FinishFill final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/finish_fill/finish_fill.hpp>``

   One finish's parameters. The defaults are the study's: its paint (#2f6e6c, a teal), its dust (#bdb6a8), a light from 125 degrees (counter-clockwise from the right) at 38 degrees above the surface, and ambient 0.32. - ``seed`` chooses the noise; equal parameters give equal bytes. - ``grain`` multiplies every feature's size, and the relief with it, so that a coarser finish keeps its slopes; within [0.5, 4]. - ``paint`` is the finish's colour; ``secondary`` the dust's (dusty matte, sand cast) or the weft's (cloth). Both are drawn opaque: their alpha is not read; the fill layer's opacity composites the finish. - For the glint, ``secondary`` is the metal's colour (the presets' own are listed with them), ``paint`` the coat's (painted sand alone draws it), ``gloss`` scales the glints' energy and ``grain`` the facets and specks; the light, ambient and relief are the preset's fitted geometry. - The light's azimuth is a whole number of degrees in [0, 359] and its elevation one in [0, 90]: the kernel reads each degree's cosine and sine from a table, never libm. - ``ambient`` in [0, 1]; ``relief`` and ``gloss`` scale the finish's own relief and sheen, within [0, 4].

   .. cpp:member:: FinishKind kind

      Default: ``{FinishKind::orange_peel}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::int32_t seed

      Default: ``{7}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double grain

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color paint

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(0x2f, 0x6e, 0x6c)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color secondary

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(0xbd, 0xb6, 0xa8)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::int32_t light_azimuth_degrees

      Default: ``{125}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::int32_t light_elevation_degrees

      Default: ``{38}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double ambient

      Default: ``{0.32}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double relief

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double gloss

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: GlintPreset glint

      Default: ``{GlintPreset::sand_blast}``.

.. cpp:struct:: ImageVariantRequest final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/primitive_registry/primitive_registry.hpp>``

   One sized variant of an image resource: the image resampled to ``extent`` device pixels, for display ``scale``. A nine-patch's fixed segments are resampled apart from its middle: ``source_slice`` are their source pixels (left, top, right, bottom) and ``fixed`` their device lengths in the variant, both zero for a stretched or tiled image, which is resampled whole. ``layer`` is the fill layer the variant serves, by index among the material's fills (an image mask's is maximum_image_variants - 1); it is not part of what the variant is. A procedural fill's pattern (E10) is asked for the same way, with ``procedural`` set and no image: the pattern generated at ``scale`` and ``extent``, never resampled. The engine never generates one inside a paint: a bake whose pattern is not ready is lent none, and the fill draws its ground; the pattern is generated in an idle frame and the parts that drew the ground are repainted.

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t layer

   .. cpp:member:: ImageId image

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t source_width

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t source_height

   .. cpp:member:: DeviceExtent extent

   .. cpp:member:: Insets source_slice

   .. cpp:member:: std::array<std::uint32_t, 4> fixed

   .. cpp:member:: DeviceScale scale

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<ProceduralFill> procedural

   One item's content to paint (owner decision W-Q6), raised while the control paints, after the item's plate and selection and before its focus cue. ``painter`` is the control's, borrowed for the call, in the control's local coordinates and clipped to ``content``; the handler draws with it and does not keep it. ``content`` is the item's content rectangle in local coordinates. ``index`` is the item's position in the control's model and ``stable_id`` its identity, borrowed for the call; ``column`` is the column of a cell in a view with columns (0 for the item's own content, n for its sub-item in column n, as Windows Forms' DrawSubItem), else 0. ``states`` are the item's anatomy states, and ``text`` and ``font`` are the colour and font the control's default content uses for them, so that owner-drawn text can match. A handler sets ``draw_default`` to have the control draw its default content over what the handler drew. A handler paints only: it must not change, dispose or re-parent the control, or anything else in its window, while the event is raised.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms::engine_light

.. cpp:class:: LightPlaneRaster final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/light_planes/light_planes.hpp>``

   -- a part's kept composite ----------------------------------------------------------------- A part composited from a bake (PartPaint::light_planes, such as IndicatorLamp's lens) keeps its last composite in one of these, owned by the part's control, so that a repaint with nothing changed (a lamp repainted for a neighbour's damage) draws the kept raster again instead of compositing. Every lamp of the bake takes the one drive. It holds the bake it composited, so a bake released elsewhere is never mistaken for a new one at the same address. One thread: the painting one.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t composites() const noexcept

      Composites made so far: one for each change of the inputs.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster> composite(const std::shared_ptr<const LightPlanes>& planes, LampDrive drive, const LightComposite& settings, DeviceExtent extent, DeviceScale scale)

      The composite of ``planes`` over ``extent`` device pixels at ``scale``, every lamp driven by ``drive``: the kept raster when the bake, drive, settings, extent and scale are those it was made with, else a new one, then kept. Null, and nothing kept, when ``planes`` is null, the extent is empty, or no raster of that extent and scale can be made (PartRaster::create). Throws std::bad_alloc when a new raster cannot be stored (what was kept stays).

   .. cpp:function:: void release() noexcept

      Releases the kept raster and bake (a control leaving its window).

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms::engine_light

.. cpp:struct:: LightPlanes final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/light_planes/light_planes.hpp>``

   One bake's planes, ``width`` × ``height`` pixels: - ``room``: the room's light, three floats a pixel (red, green, blue), premultiplied by coverage; - ``lamps``: ``lamp_count`` planes of the same shape, one after another, each lamp alone at unit white power; - ``coverage``: one float a pixel in [0, 1], the share of the pixel the key covers. Rows run top to bottom, pixels left to right.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t width

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t height

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t lamp_count

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<float> room

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<float> lamps

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<float> coverage

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms::engine_light

.. cpp:struct:: LitKeyBake final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/light_planes/light_planes.hpp>``

   -- the lit keys' bake ------------------------------------------------------------------ One lit key's bake parameters, and the values the study baked its dialog keys with (lamp_keys.cpp, the dialog family): - height above the panel as a share of the key's short side: 0.57 there, an early test value. Relief is contextual (owner, 2026-11-12): each role takes its own height, a theme parameter, so the baker is given the role's share and a bake is made per role; nothing here fixes one; - the sides lean in by 3° + 17° × clamp((share − 0.25) / 0.75, 0, 1); - the sides are the key's own plastic; - the control camera tilted 16° toward the viewer; - OK lit as the ANTI-ICE caption: clear acrylic, its one coil just above a frosted back, so the coil is neither invisible nor plainly visible; CANCEL in white opaque plastic with its lamps deep in the shell; - renders 240 pixels wide, shown 180 logical units wide; - composited with the room at 1 (panel_lamps' colouring). Light is never local (owner, 2026-11-12): a lamp lights its own lens and legend only. The composite keeps it so, writing nothing where the key's coverage is 0, so a bake's spill onto the panel never shows. The baker is run by the integrator (``lamps <dir> 240 dialog``); nothing at run time reads these but the display width.

   .. cpp:member:: double height_share

   .. cpp:member:: double camera_tilt_degrees

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t bake_width

   .. cpp:member:: double display_width

   .. cpp:member:: double room

      Default: ``{MarkerShape::circle}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialAssemblyContext final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   Replaces ``material`` with ``floor`` and the declarations of ``fields`` (the winning declaration of each field, by the field's position, null where none) in place: a part's assembled material (EC1); an ``initial`` declaration keeps the floor's value. The fills are composed when any fill field is declared: the colour layer (the declared colour, else the floor's bottom layer when it is solid) under the image layers (the declared ones, each authored gradient resolved for ``box``, else the floor's others), which take the declared blend modes, and whose image fills take the declared sizes (resolved for ``box``), then repetitions, then positions; a colour of alpha 0 adds no layer, and with no layer at all the fill is one transparent solid. More than SurfaceMaterial::maximum_fill_layers is no material. The corners are composed when any is declared: each is its declaration's, else the floor's (its corner_radii's, else its corner_radius); four equal corners are corner_radius alone, and others corner_radii, with corner_radius the smallest (the built-in path rounds every corner by it). The glows take the declared placements; a mask's authored gradient is resolved for ``box``. The border's edges are assembled together when any of their fields is declared: each starts as the floor's (its single edge, else its uniform border; an edge the floor lacks has the default MaterialBorder's width and colour and is not drawn), takes its declared style, width and colour, and is drawn unless its style is none or its width 0. Four equal edges are drawn as one uniform border, which keeps a dash and rounded corners, and unequal ones as single edges, which a material admits only with square corners (valid_surface_material); an edge drawn as two lines (MaterialEdgeLines) is not a border edge but its two keylines, after the declared ones. A border image is the top fill layer, and the border (its edges and their lines) is not drawn, as CSS draws a border image in place of the border's style. ``box`` is the part's box in logical units: relative corners are fractions of its shorter side. What a part gives its material's relative values (P8c): its em, and its text colour, which CSS's currentcolor names.

   .. cpp:member:: double em

   .. cpp:member:: Color current_colour

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialBlocks final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_engine/theme_engine.hpp>``

   A material repeated over an area (ThemeEngine::paint_material_blocks): - ``key`` names the part's record (borrowed for the call); - ``material`` is borrowed for the call; - ``period`` is the material's repeat in logical units (a procedural fill's tile), within [minimum_procedural_tile, maximum_procedural_tile]; - the blocks lie on the lattice of whole periods through ``anchor``, so that they stay where the caller anchors them as it pans, and are drawn where they meet ``area``, clipped to it (the painter's coordinates).

   .. cpp:member:: PartKey key

   .. cpp:member:: const SurfaceMaterial* material

   .. cpp:member:: double period

   .. cpp:member:: Point anchor

   .. cpp:member:: Rect area

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialBorderEdges final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/types/surface_material_types.hpp>``

   Single edges (CSS border-color with differing sides). On a square box each is a band along its edge; on a rounded one (corner_radius above 0, every present edge the same width) they draw as one ring, each pixel in the colour of the edge it lies nearest (split along 45-degree lines from the corners, as CSS splits a rounded box's edge colours), and the material's keylines draw as rings the same way.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: MaterialColourFilter final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/colour_matrix/colour_matrix.hpp>``

   A material's colour filter: none, or one matrix held out of line. Every recipe of every theme holds a SurfaceMaterial, so a matrix held inline (160 bytes) would grow a theme definition by tens of kilobytes; this holder is one pointer. It owns its matrix; a copy copies the matrix, a move leaves the source empty, and two filters are equal when both are empty or their matrices are equal.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: MaterialColourFilter() noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: MaterialColourFilter(const MaterialColourMatrix& matrix)

      Implicit, as std::optional's is: ``material.colour_filter = matrix;``. Throws std::bad_alloc when the matrix cannot be held.

   .. cpp:function:: MaterialColourFilter(const MaterialColourFilter& other)

   .. cpp:function:: MaterialColourFilter(MaterialColourFilter&& other) noexcept


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: bool has_value() const noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

      Leaves the filter empty.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialColourMatrix final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/colour_matrix/colour_matrix.hpp>``

   A colour filter (E9.6; CSS ``filter`` and ``backdrop-filter`` colour functions): a 4 × 5 matrix on straight (not premultiplied) colour with each channel in [0, 1]. Its rows give red, green, blue and alpha; its columns read red, green, blue and alpha, and the fifth is a constant. Each result is clamped to [0, 1]. The factories are the CSS functions, with CSS's matrices (Filter Effects Level 1). A list of functions composes into one matrix with :cpp:func:`then <gui_forms::MaterialColourMatrix::then>`, in source order. CSS clamps between the functions of a list; one matrix does not, which differs only where an intermediate colour leaves [0, 1].


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix brightness(double amount)

      Each amount is clamped as CSS clamps it: brightness, contrast and saturate to at least 0; grayscale, sepia, invert and opacity to [0, 1]. A non-finite amount throws std::invalid_argument.

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix contrast(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix saturate(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix grayscale(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix sepia(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix invert(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix opacity(double amount)

   .. cpp:function:: static MaterialColourMatrix hue_rotate(double degrees)

      Turns hues by ``degrees`` (any finite angle), with the engine's sine and cosine so that the matrix has the same bits on every host.

   .. cpp:function:: MaterialColourMatrix then(const MaterialColourMatrix& after) const noexcept

      This filter, then ``after``: one matrix applying both.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t rows

      Default: ``4U``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t columns

      Default: ``5U``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::array<double, rows * columns> values

      Row-major: values[row × 5 + column]. The identity by default. Default: ``{detail::identity_colour_entries}``.

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialDeclaration final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   One material field a rule sets and its value. Only the members ``field`` names are read; the others stay empty: - background_colour: ``colour``, a solid layer under the image layers; - fills: the image layers (gradients, images, patterns), bottom first, at most SurfaceMaterial::maximum_fill_layers, empty for none; and ``gradients``, empty, or one per layer: a gradient as a sheet wrote it (T3 P6b), resolved for the part's box when the material is assembled, in place of its layer; an entry of kind ``solid`` keeps its layer as written; - fill_blend_modes: ``blend_modes``, in CSS's order (the top image layer's first), repeated over the image layers as CSS repeats a short list; empty for normal; - fill_positions, fill_sizes, fill_repeats: ``image_positions``, ``image_sizes``, ``image_repeats``, in the same order and repeated the same way, read by the image fills (not a nine-patch) among the image layers: a gradient layer covers the box; - shadows, glows, keylines: the lists; - glow_placements: ``glow_placements``, in the glows' order, repeated over them as CSS repeats a short list (``glow-placement``); - an edge's style: ``border_style``, empty for none (CSS's ``none``), and ``edge_lines``; its width: ``number``, 0 for none; its colour: ``colour``; - border_image: ``border_image``, a nine-patch image fill, empty for none; - a corner: ``number``, its radius in logical units, or, with ``relative``, a fraction of the box's shorter side (CSS's percentage, R2-11), resolved when the material is assembled for a part; - colour_filter: ``colour_filter``; drop_shadow: ``drop_shadow``, empty for none; mask: ``mask``, empty for none, and ``gradients``, empty or one entry, the mask's authored gradient as for a fill layer; - filter_blur and finish: ``number``; - reflection: ``reflection``, empty for none (stage T5, C7); - seat: ``number``, the part's height above its panel in logical units (negative: sunk into it), or, with ``relative``, a fraction of the box's shorter side, and ``colour``, its shadow's; 0 for none. Resolved when the material is assembled (seat_shadows). ``initial``, set, is CSS's ``initial``: the field keeps the floor's value, and the declaration still wins over those beneath it; its value members are not read.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialGlow final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/types/surface_material_types.hpp>``

   A soft coloured emission of the material's box (E9-C): the box grown by ``spread`` on every side and blurred by ``blur_radius`` (σ = blur_radius / 2, as a shadow's), centred on the box. The engine's material bake adds it as light, each premultiplied channel at most 1: - ``under``, to the outer shadows beneath it, with the fills drawn over it. - ``over``, to the finished face (after its keylines), within the box's coverage. paint_surface_material adds them so too where it bakes through the kernel. A minimal painter's layers composite each as an outer shadow at no offset (an over glow clipped to the face, after the keylines): that is source-over, not added, so its under glow differs where it lies over a shadow and its over glow is the dimmer. Its reach, spread + 2 × blur_radius (where its blur is below one 8-bit step), is at most SurfaceMaterial::maximum_glow_reach in both placements, so that an under glow stays within the part's granted overflow; an over glow lies within the box and needs no overflow.

   .. cpp:member:: Color color

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(255, 255, 255, 96)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double blur_radius

      Default: ``{2.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double spread

   .. cpp:member:: MaterialGlowPlacement placement

      Default: ``{MaterialGlowPlacement::under}``.

.. cpp:struct:: MaterialReflection final

The reflection is composited beneath what the face drew past that edge (its outer shadows and glows). The engine's material primitive draws it, and omits it under high contrast and reduced transparency (T5-Q16); the built-in path, paint_surface_material, draws none.

The resolution bounds are inclusive, in device pixels per logical unit; each is finite and above zero.
