.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: MotionClock final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_clock/motion_clock.hpp>``

   One window's motion clock (T2 plan, MotionClock; GIVEN D1-1). The window owns it; it observes the window. It holds at most one frame request, armed only while some value it holds has not settled, however many values move; the request is disconnected once the last one settles, so a window at rest schedules no frames. Execution: every member runs on the window's UI thread. Frames, settling and the listeners' damage all happen inside the window's frame poll. Event order on a frame: each value enlisted before the frame starts is advanced to the frame's time, in the order of its record's slot (the same for the same sequence of starts and releases); a value that settles is released before its listener runs; then the owner is invalidated as the value declared, then its listener runs. Values started by a listener are first advanced on the next frame. A listener that throws is counted as a frame callback fault; its value jumps to its end. Ending motion (all by name): - finish_all: reduced motion turned on (GIVEN D2-1) or the window occluded or minimized (GIVEN D4-1). Every value jumps to its end and its listener runs once, with the cause. While either holds, a new move jumps to its end at once and nothing is scheduled. - release_owner: a control left the window. Its values jump to their ends without listeners. - cancel: the window closes (or cancels its frame requests). Every value jumps to its end without listeners, and the request is disconnected. A record is named by its handle; a stale handle (its record released) is rejected, so a value whose owner has gone is never advanced. Ambient effects (T2 plan, phase E7; design language §9.4) share the same request. The engine enlists a part with an ambient effect while it draws it; on each frame at least the effect's frame interval after its last, the part's extent repaints. An effect whose part was not repainted since its last frame (the part is no longer shown) is dropped, so an effect can never keep a window painting by itself. finish_all and cancel drop every effect too; finish_all repaints each part once, at its static pose. A control hosting an effect program (a progress bar's stripes or bubbles) enlists itself the same way (enlist_control_ambient): on each frame due, its on_ambient_frame advances and invalidates it, and a control that was not painted since its last frame is dropped. The clock observes it: one that died or left the window is dropped unasked. finish_all and stop park the controls (each repaints once, at its still pose, under finish_all); repaint_parked repaints them when ambient motion may resume, and their paints enlist them again.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: explicit MotionClock(SurfaceRoot& root) noexcept


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: FrameTime now() const noexcept

      The time a move starting now starts at.

   .. cpp:function:: bool armed() const noexcept

      Whether the clock holds its frame request.

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t moving_count() const noexcept

      Values moving on the clock now.

   .. cpp:function:: bool suppressed() const noexcept

      Whether a move would jump to its end at once: reduced motion, or an occluded or minimized window.

   .. cpp:function:: bool ambient_suppressed() const noexcept

      Whether ambient effects are stopped: as suppressed(), or the application's motion switch off (PresentationSettings::ambient_motion).

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t ambient_count() const noexcept

      Ambient effects running on the clock now (parts' and controls').


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: bool enlist_control_ambient(Control& control, FrameInterval interval)

      A control's ambient effect, its frames at least ``interval`` apart (see the class comment). Returns true when the control was not enlisted (its effect's time starts now), false when it was (the interval is replaced). Nothing is enlisted, and false returned, while ambient motion is suppressed. If the record's place or the frame request cannot be made, the exception propagates and nothing changes. The control must be held by a shared pointer and attached to this clock's window.

   .. cpp:function:: void withdraw_control_ambient(const Control& control) noexcept

      Ends the control's ambient effect, if any (parked too). Never fails.

   .. cpp:function:: bool control_ambient(const Control& control) const noexcept

      Whether the control is enlisted now.

   .. cpp:function:: void set_time_source(const MotionTimeSource* source) noexcept

      Borrows ``source`` (it must outlive its installation); null restores the steady clock.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MotionCurve final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

   How a value moves. Make it with the named factories, which keep the fields that belong together consistent.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve timed(FrameInterval duration, MotionEasing easing) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve cubic_bezier(FrameInterval duration, double x1, double y1, double x2, double y2) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve stepped(FrameInterval duration, std::uint32_t steps, MotionStepPosition position = MotionStepPosition::end) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve follow(FrameInterval time_constant = follow_time_constant) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve spring(FrameInterval response, double overshoot = 0.0) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: static MotionCurve decay(FrameInterval time_constant) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: MotionCurve delayed(FrameInterval delay) const noexcept

      This curve with ``delay`` before each move, clamped as ``delay`` is.

   .. cpp:member:: MotionKind kind

      Default: ``{MotionKind::timed}``.

   .. cpp:member:: FrameInterval duration

      timed: the duration. follow and decay: the time constant. spring: the response time, 1/ω for the natural angular frequency ω. Clamped to [0, motion_duration_ceiling]; zero means the value jumps to its end. Default: ``{std::chrono::milliseconds(180)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: MotionEasing easing

      timed only. Default: ``{MotionEasing::smoothstep}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double overshoot

      spring only: the overshoot of a move from rest, as a fraction of the distance, from 0 (critically damped) to maximum_motion_overshoot.

   .. cpp:member:: std::array<double, 4> bezier

      timed with cubic_bezier: x1, y1, x2, y2. Each x is clamped to [0, 1] and each y to [−maximum_motion_overshoot, 1 + maximum_motion_overshoot], so the curve overshoots by at most 8 %; a non-finite point is the linear curve's. Default: ``{0.0, 0.0, 1.0, 1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t steps

      timed with steps: the levels, clamped to [1, maximum_motion_steps], and where they jump. Default: ``{1U}``.

   .. cpp:member:: MotionStepPosition step_position

      Default: ``{MotionStepPosition::end}``.

   .. cpp:member:: FrameInterval delay

      Every curve (E9.6, CSS transition-delay): how long a move holds the value still before it starts, at no velocity. Clamped to [0, the ceiling less the duration], so that a delayed move still ends within motion_duration_ceiling.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MotionRecordHandle final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

   Which record of a clock a value holds while it moves: a slot and the slot's generation when the record was made. A released record's handle is stale and names nothing, even after its slot is reused.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t slot

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t generation

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MotionStep final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

   One step of a value, as its listener sees it. ``value`` is the value that moved; it is valid for the call.

   .. cpp:member:: MotionValue* value

   .. cpp:member:: double previous

   .. cpp:member:: double current

   .. cpp:member:: bool settled

      The value reached its settled condition (or jumped to its end) and stands exactly at its target; the clock no longer advances it.

   .. cpp:member:: MotionStepCause cause

      Default: ``{MotionStepCause::advanced}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: MotionStop final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

   One intermediate stop of a timed move (E9.6, multi-stop @keyframes): the value passes ``value`` at ``offset`` of the move's duration, 0 < offset < 1. Between stops the move is eased by its curve's easing.

   .. cpp:member:: double offset

   .. cpp:member:: double value

   .. cpp:member:: double value_em

      The value's em coefficient (T3 P8c): a theme's stop is ``value`` plus the part's em times it; the theme engine resolves it before the stop reaches a motion value, which reads ``value`` alone.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: MotionTimeSource

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_clock/motion_clock.hpp>``

   Where a clock reads the time a motion starts. Steady-clock time unless a source is installed: tests install one so that motion is sampled from a time they choose, never from the wall clock. Its times must be in the same domain as the times the window is polled with.


   .. rubric:: Extension points

   .. cpp:function:: virtual FrameTime now() const noexcept = 0

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: MotionValue final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

   One value. Ownership: a control owns it as a member (or through something the control owns), and names itself as the owner, so the owner outlives it. It neither copies nor moves, so the clock's record of it stays valid; it releases that record when it is destroyed. All of it runs on the UI thread of the owner's window. The settled conditions, in device pixels at the window's scale (extent times scale converts value units): - timed: the duration has elapsed; - follow and decay: the remaining gap is within motion_settle_tolerance; - spring: the gap is within it, and so is the movement over one 60 Hz frame at the current velocity. On settling the value snaps exactly to its target, so the last frame is the static rendering. Every move starts from where the value stands at that moment, with its velocity: a retarget mid-flight never jumps (design language §9.1).


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: MotionValue(Control& owner, MotionCurve curve, double initial = 0.0) noexcept

      A settled value at ``initial``, owned by :cpp:func:`owner <gui_forms::MotionValue::owner>`.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: double current() const noexcept

      The value at the last frame, start or jump.

   .. cpp:function:: double target() const noexcept

      Where the value is going (a decay: where it comes to rest).

   .. cpp:function:: double velocity() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: bool settled() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: const MotionCurve& curve() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: void set_curve(MotionCurve curve) noexcept

      The curve of the next move; a move under way keeps its own. Durations and overshoot are clamped (see MotionCurve).

   .. cpp:function:: double extent() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: bool set_extent(double logical_units) noexcept

      The logical length one unit of the value spans, for the settled conditions (default 1: the value is a logical length). A progress across a 150-unit slide has extent 150. Refused (returns false, the extent unchanged) outside [minimum_motion_extent, maximum_motion_extent] or when not finite.

   .. cpp:function:: Control& owner() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: bool enlisted() const noexcept

      Whether a clock holds this value's record (it is moving on it).


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: MotionStart animate_to(double target)

      Moves toward :cpp:func:`target <gui_forms::MotionValue::target>` from where the value stands now. A decay comes to rest at the target. If the clock's record or its frame request cannot be made, the exception propagates and the value is unchanged.

   .. cpp:function:: MotionStart animate_through(std::span<const MotionStop> stops, double target)

      A timed move from where the value stands now through ``stops`` to :cpp:func:`target <gui_forms::MotionValue::target>` (E9.6): each stop's offset is a fraction of the duration, strictly increasing within (0, 1). It starts a new move even toward the target already set. Refused, the value unchanged, for a curve that is not timed, more than maximum_motion_stops stops, an offset out of order or out of range, or a value or target that is not finite. While it runs, animate_to the same target leaves it running.

   .. cpp:function:: MotionStart fling(double velocity)

      decay only: launches the value at :cpp:func:`velocity <gui_forms::MotionValue::velocity>` (value units per second); it comes to rest where friction stops it.

   .. cpp:function:: MotionStart adopt(const MotionValue& source)

      Moves as ``source`` moves: its curve, its current move and its time, on this value's own clock (a docking guest showing another window's stamp). A settled source makes this value jump to its value.

   .. cpp:function:: void finish() noexcept

      Ends the move: the value stands at its target, settled. No listener runs.

   .. cpp:function:: void jump_to(double value) noexcept

      Stands the value at ``value``, settled (a non-finite value is ignored). No listener runs.

   .. cpp:function:: void set_listener(MotionListener listener) noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: void set_invalidation(Dirty dirty) noexcept

      What the clock invalidates on the owner after each step; none by default (the listener invalidates what it moved).

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PaintCornerRadii final

   ``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_types/paint_types.hpp>``

   A rounded rectangle's four corner radii, in the rectangle's units: top-left, top-right, bottom-right and bottom-left. A painter draws each at most half the rectangle's shorter side.

   .. cpp:member:: double top_left

   .. cpp:member:: double top_right

   .. cpp:member:: double bottom_right

   .. cpp:member:: double bottom_left

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: Painter : public TextMetricsProvider

   ``#include <gui_forms/types/painter/painter.hpp>``

   Painter is the renderer-neutral command vocabulary for transforms, clips, solids, rounded geometry, gradients, shadows, text, registered images, live surfaces, sampled regions, and bounded patterns; rich operations provide coherent minimal-host fallbacks.


   .. rubric:: Extension points

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void begin_group(const PaintGroup& group)

      Paint groups (see PaintGroup). begin_group and end_group pair like save and restore and nest like them, up to maximum_paint_group_depth: the state in force at end_group (transform, clips, saves) must be the one at its begin_group. Within a retained display chunk every group ends in the chunk that begins it; the recorder refuses a chunk left unbalanced. The recording and Skia painters isolate; each keeps one reusable scratch image per nesting level, grown to the largest clipped bounds it has met and released with the painter. The base implementation is a minimal painter's fallback without isolation: save() and clip_rect(bounds), then restore().

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void end_group()

   .. cpp:function:: virtual std::unique_ptr<PaintFramebuffer> create_framebuffer(Size logical_size, double scale)

      Native renderers preserve their fonts and drawing semantics offscreen. Recording/headless painters explicitly report this facility unavailable.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual PreparedTextPaintResult draw_prepared_text(const PreparedTextLayout& layout, const LayoutAuthority expected, const Point baseline, const Color color)

      Development surface: recording retains immutable storage; a compatible backend stages pixels. Only the host's successful frame commit presents.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void save() = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void restore() = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void translate(Point offset) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void clip_rect(Rect rect) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void clip_rounded_rect(Rect rect, double radius)

      Rich geometry has deterministic renderer-neutral fallbacks so a minimal host stays coherent. Recording and production raster painters override these operations to preserve the authored material exactly.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void clip_rounded_rect_corners(Rect rect, PaintCornerRadii radii)

      Narrows the clip to ``rect`` with each corner rounded by its own radius (EP-6: an effect clipped to its part's shape). The fallback rounds every corner by the largest of the four, so it never shows more than the exact clip at a corner.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_rect(Rect rect, Color color) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_rounded_rect(Rect rect, double radius, Color color)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void stroke_rect(Rect rect, Color color, double width) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void stroke_rounded_rect(Rect rect, double radius, Color color, double width)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_linear_gradient(Rect rect, Point start, Point end, std::span<const GradientStop> stops)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_linear_gradient_spread(Rect rect, Point start, Point end, std::span<const GradientStop> stops, GradientSpreadMode spread)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_radial_gradient(Rect rect, Point center, Size radii, std::span<const GradientStop> stops)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_box_shadow(Rect rect, double corner_radius, Point offset, double blur_radius, double spread, Color color)

      Drawn before the owning surface fill. The shape body may therefore be included by a fallback without changing the final composited result.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_retained_box_shadow(Rect rect, double corner_radius, Point offset, double blur_radius, double spread, Color color, const std::shared_ptr<RetainedDrawCache>& cache)

      Replay of a retained draw_box_shadow. A renderer may keep the blurred coverage in the cache; the default draws as draw_box_shadow.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_inset_box_shadow(Rect rect, double corner_radius, Point offset, double blur_radius, double spread, Color color)

      Drawn over the owning surface fill and clipped to its interior. The source is the complement of a translated, spread-adjusted inner box, so the recipe relaxes with live owner geometry rather than fixed traces.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_line(Point from, Point to, Color color, double width) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void stroke_polyline(std::span<const Point> points, const StrokeStyle& style)

      Batched marks (IMGUI_IMPLOT_STUDY P7). Each draws a whole caller-owned span as one command: the painter reads the span during the call only and allocates nothing per element; a recording copies the span once. Invalid styles, and points or rectangles that are not finite where a call says so, draw nothing. The base implementations are a minimal painter's fallbacks built from draw_line, fill_rect and the other primitives (solid strokes, square joins, no anti-aliasing of their own); the recording and Skia painters draw them exactly. An open polyline through ``points``, joined and capped by ``style``. A point whose coordinate is NaN breaks the line (a gap): each run of finite points between breaks is stroked on its own, and a run of one point draws nothing. An infinite coordinate is refused (the call draws nothing).

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_polygon(std::span<const Point> points, Color color, PolygonFillRule rule)

      A closed polygon through ``points`` (at least 3, all finite), filled by ``rule``.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_band(std::span<const Point> upper, std::span<const Point> lower, Color color)

      The area between two polylines sampled at the same positions (a plot's shaded band, area or stacked area): ``upper`` and ``lower`` hold the same count, at least 2, all finite; the band's outline runs along ``upper`` and back along ``lower``, filled nonzero. A constant baseline is a ``lower`` of equal points.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_rects(std::span<const Rect> rects, Color color)

      Each rectangle filled in one colour (bars, histogram bins). Empty or non-finite rectangles are skipped.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_rects_each(std::span<const Rect> rects, std::span<const Color> colors)

      Each rectangle filled in its own colour: ``colors`` holds one per rectangle (heatmap cells drawn as rectangles). Mismatched counts draw nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void stroke_segments(std::span<const PaintSegment> segments, const StrokeStyle& style)

      Independent segments with ``style``'s caps and dashes and no joins (error bars, stems, ticks, grid lines). Segments with a non-finite end are skipped.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_markers(std::span<const Point> centers, const MarkerStyle& style)

      A marker centred on each point (scatter, line markers, decorators). Points with a non-finite coordinate are skipped.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_conic_gradient(Rect rect, Point center, double start_degrees, std::span<const GradientStop> stops)

      A conic (sweep) gradient over ``rect`` (piece C9: a hue wheel): the colour at a point is the stops' colour at the angle about ``center``, measured clockwise (y down) from ``start_degrees`` (0 along +x), one full turn from offset 0 to offset 1. Stops as valid_gradient_stops says; the base implementation fills 90 wedges in the stops' colours.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_checker(Rect rect, const CheckerPattern& pattern)

      ``rect`` filled with a checkerboard (piece C7). A cell narrower than one unit, or a rectangle holding more than maximum_checker_cells cells, fills the rectangle with the average of the two colours instead.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_grid_pattern(Rect rect, const GridPattern& pattern)

      The grid lines of ``pattern`` that cross ``rect``, clipped to it (piece C17). Refused patterns (valid_grid_pattern) draw nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_rounded_rect_corners(Rect rect, PaintCornerRadii radii, Color color)

      A rounded rectangle with each corner rounded by its own radius (piece C1; joined segments round only a row's outer ends). The base implementation fills under clip_rounded_rect_corners; the stroke's draws stroke_rounded_rect with the largest radius.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void stroke_rounded_rect_corners(Rect rect, PaintCornerRadii radii, Color color, double width)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_text_utf8(Point origin, std::string_view text, FontSpec font, Color color) = 0

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_retained_text_utf8(Point origin, std::string_view text, FontSpec font, Color color, const std::shared_ptr<RetainedDrawCache>& cache)

      Replay of a retained draw_text_utf8. The cache lives as long as the recorded command; a renderer may keep shaped text there. The default draws as draw_text_utf8 and leaves the cache untouched.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_turned_text_utf8(Point origin, std::string_view text, FontSpec font, Color color, TextOrientation orientation)

      One line of text turned a quarter about ``origin`` (see TextOrientation). ``origin`` is the start of the baseline, as for draw_text_utf8; the run advances up the surface (bottom_to_top) or down it (top_to_bottom), so the measured width of the text becomes its height. ``horizontal`` draws as draw_text_utf8. The base implementation is a minimal painter's fallback that draws the run horizontally from ``origin``, unturned; the recording and Skia painters turn it.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_glyph_runs(Point origin, const GlyphRunsView& view, FontSpec font, Color color)

      One line of glyph runs a provider shaped earlier (GlyphRunsView, as a paragraph layout fills them), drawn at ``origin`` (the start of the baseline, as for draw_text_utf8) in ``font``'s size and colour without shaping again. A painter whose own engine shaped them, in its current face table (view.source), draws exactly the pixels draw_text_utf8 draws for view.text; for any other source, or runs whose glyph indices fall outside view.glyphs, it draws view.text with draw_text_utf8. That fallback is the base implementation, which the recording painter keeps (a recorded line is shaped again on replay); the Skia painter draws the runs.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual Size measure_text_utf8(std::string_view text, FontSpec font)

      Text controls need the same metrics used by the painter for caret hit testing, selection geometry, and horizontal viewport maintenance. The renderer owns those metrics; controls must not guess a fixed glyph width.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedTextLayout resolve_text_layout_utf8(std::string_view text, FontSpec font) override

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_image(ImageId image, Rect destination, double opacity = 1.0) = 0

      Draws ``image`` over ``destination``. Below 1x on either axis the CPU renderer draws a copy resampled by pixel area (stx_conv) to the device size, cached by image content and size; at or above 1x it draws the image directly with a linear filter.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_live_surface(std::shared_ptr<LiveSurface> surface, Rect destination, double opacity = 1.0)

      A live raster is a retained renderer-neutral resource whose producer may publish independently of the UI dispatcher. Display lists retain the resource and terminal renderers sample only its newest complete frame.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual Rect opaque_live_surface_cover(const std::shared_ptr<LiveSurface>& surface, Rect destination, double opacity)

      The part of ``destination``, in the current coordinates, that draw_live_surface(surface, destination, opacity) would overwrite with opaque pixels if drawn now, whatever lay beneath: replay then need not paint what an opaque live surface hides (a panned canvas's sheet). Empty when the painter cannot promise it (the default): the newest frame is not opaque, the opacity is below one, the transform is not a scale and translation, or a clip has soft edges. Reads only.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_part_raster(const std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster>& raster, Point origin, double opacity = 1.0)

      Composites one of the theme engine's part rasters (T2 plan, E2; GIVEN A2-2 and C1-1). ``origin`` is the raster's top-left corner in logical units; the painter places the raster's first pixel on the device pixel nearest to it, one raster pixel per device pixel, so the part is device-aligned and never resampled. ``opacity`` in [0, 1] is the part's opacity, one multiplier on its whole composite. The blend is the shared premultiplied source-over (detail::composite_part_raster) under the current clip, so every host writes the same bytes. A raster made for another display scale than the painter's is not drawn: the engine rasterizes a part for the scale it is shown at. The base implementation draws nothing; the recording and Skia painters override it. A null raster draws nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_part_raster_scaled(const std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster>& raster, Rect destination, double opacity = 1.0)

      Composites a part raster shown at another size (E7r): the raster is stretched over ``destination``, in logical units, whose corners the painter places on the device pixels nearest them. Each device pixel samples the raster bilinearly through the same shared blend, so every host writes the same bytes. The engine shows a part this way while its presentational scale moves, and bakes it sharp at its size once the scale rests. Otherwise as draw_part_raster; the base implementation draws nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_surface_material(Rect bounds, const SurfaceMaterial& material)

      Draws a valid, unfinished ``material`` over ``bounds`` (paint_surface_material calls it after its checks). Painters that can composite part rasters draw it as one (stage T4, owner 2026-11-07: one rasteriser): the theme engine's material kernel bakes it at the painter's display scale and the raster goes on the device pixels nearest the box grown by its outsets, so a material looks the same whether a rule selects it as a part's face or a control paints it. The base implementation, a minimal painter's fallback, draws its layers as fills, strokes and shadows, in the kernel's order.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_baked_surface_material(Rect bounds, const std::shared_ptr<const SurfaceMaterial>& material, const std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster>& raster, double outset)

      The replay of a material a recording baked: ``raster``, made at the recording's display scale, covers ``bounds`` grown by ``outset`` logical units on every side. A painter at that scale composites it as draw_part_raster does; one at another scale, or one that cannot composite rasters, draws the material as draw_surface_material does. The base implementation calls draw_surface_material. ``material`` is shared read-only with the recording; null draws nothing.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_image_region(ImageId image, Rect source, Rect destination, double opacity = 1.0)

      Source-region replay is the renderer-neutral primitive behind image strips, texture fills, and nine-patch materials. ``source`` is expressed in source-image pixels. Minimal painters may preserve coherence by falling back to whole-image scaling; production painters override it.

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void draw_image_region_sampled(ImageId image, Rect source, Rect destination, ImageSampling sampling, double opacity = 1.0)

   .. cpp:function:: virtual void fill_image_pattern(ImageId image, Size source_pixel_size, Rect destination, Size logical_tile_size, ImagePatternWrap wrap = ImagePatternWrap::tile, double opacity = 1.0)

      One retained command regardless of repetition count. Production painters realize the pattern exactly; the base implementation is a bounded compatibility fallback for deliberately minimal hosts.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PaintGroup final

   ``#include <gui_forms/types/painter/painter.hpp>``

   A paint group (T2 plan, E7; owner, 2026-10-21: "there are other things that group layer may be needed for"): commands drawn as one image, then composited once. - ``bounds``, in the current coordinates when the group begins, limits the group. It is given up front so that a painter sizes its storage once: nothing the group draws outside ``bounds`` (or outside the clip in force at its beginning) is shown. - ``opacity`` in [0, 1] multiplies the group's whole image. Below 1 the group is isolated: its commands draw into a transparent premultiplied image the size of its clipped bounds, which is composited once at ``opacity`` by ``composite``, so overlapping commands never show through one another. At 1 (or above) the commands draw directly under clip_rect(bounds), which is exactly what isolation would give for source-over. At 0 (or NaN) nothing is drawn. - A group whose ``composite`` is not source-over, or that sets ``isolated``, is isolated at any opacity: a group holding a plus group must be, so that the sum is formed before it meets what lies beneath. A painter that cannot isolate (no storage) draws the group directly.

   .. cpp:member:: Rect bounds

   .. cpp:member:: double opacity

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: PaintGroupComposite composite

      Default: ``{PaintGroupComposite::source_over}``.

   .. cpp:member:: bool isolated

      Default: ``{false}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PaintSegment final

   ``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_marks/paint_marks.hpp>``

   One independent segment of stroke_segments (no joins between segments).

   .. cpp:member:: Point from

   .. cpp:member:: Point to

.. cpp:struct:: PartArc final

An arc runs from ``start`` through ``sweep`` (negative sweeps anticlockwise; at most one turn either way is drawn); a needle points at ``start`` and reads no sweep.

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_shape/part_shape.hpp>``

   A part's hit region (T1's hand-off): its shape in its bounds, never less than the minimum target. A point hits when it lies in the bounds and either inside the shape or inside the minimum target centred in the bounds (clipped to them), so a hit region may exceed what is drawn but never falls below the minimum.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PartKey final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/types/engine_types.hpp>``

   A painted part's identity from frame to frame (T2 GIVEN H3-1), which lets the engine keep a record for it: its states and when it entered them, its transitions, its preparation and its cached raster. The full identity is the control's stable ID, the declared part, and for an item part the item's stable ID. The engine takes the control's stable ID from the control painting the part, so a key cannot name another control; the key carries the rest: - ``part``: the part's declared name, as in the control's anatomy; - ``item``: the item's stable ID, empty for a part that is not an item's or for an item without one; - ``item_index``: the item's index. It is part of the identity only when ``item`` is empty: the index fallback, which the window counts in its metrics, because the record then follows the position, not the item. Every view is borrowed for the call that paints the part.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: constexpr bool index_fallback() const noexcept

      Whether the key falls back to an item index.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string_view part

   .. cpp:member:: std::string_view item

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<std::size_t> item_index

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: PartMotion final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

   A part's running transitions (E7; T2 plan, "How motion is driven"). The part's record owns it through a pointer, so that its motion values, which never move, keep their addresses however the table's storage grows. Each value is owned by the part's control (MotionValue's owner), runs on the window's MotionClock, and reports each step to on_step, which repaints the part's extent on the control that recorded it. The record is released with its control, which releases the values from the clock.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PartPaint final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/part_painting/part_painting.hpp>``

   One part to paint, in the control's local coordinates. ``text``, ``image`` and ``mark`` are read only by roles that show them (label, caption and content; icon and image; glyph and indicator). ``text`` is lent for the call (lend_paint_text). ``font`` is an effective font (Control::effective_font). ``corner_radius``, when set, gives a surface part its shape; the theme (T4) will own shapes, so a control sets it only where today's recipe has no shape for the part. ``orientation`` and ``alignment`` are read with ``text``; ``dock_region`` only by the dock_shape mark.

   .. cpp:member:: PartRole role

      Default: ``{PartRole::region}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Rect bounds

   .. cpp:member:: AnatomyStateSet states

   .. cpp:member:: ControlVisualRole family

      Default: ``{ControlVisualRole::panel}``.

   .. cpp:member:: PartMark mark

      Default: ``{PartMark::none}``.

   .. cpp:member:: BorrowedText text

   .. cpp:member:: FontSpec font

   .. cpp:member:: PartTextAlignment alignment

      Default: ``{PartTextAlignment::start}``.

   .. cpp:member:: TextOrientation orientation

      The text's reading direction (§8.5; owner decision DK-35, J4). A turned label lies along the part's height, its line centred across the width; ``alignment`` then runs from the end reading starts at (the bottom for bottom_to_top, the top for top_to_bottom). Default: ``{TextOrientation::horizontal}``.

   .. cpp:member:: PartTextTone tone

      The text's tone: ordinary text in the recipe's text colour, secondary text in its muted ink. Read only with ``text``. Default: ``{PartTextTone::ordinary}``.

   .. cpp:member:: DockShapeRegion dock_region

      The region the dock_shape mark fills. Default: ``{DockShapeRegion::tab_into}``.

   .. cpp:member:: ImageId image

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<double> corner_radius

      A focus or preselect ring follows the same shape: a connected segment's square corners, for one.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<double> point

      A pointed face (design language §8.5; owner decision 2026-11-02): the surface's trailing end is a nose this many units long, its tip at the middle of the right edge, as a raised breadcrumb segment's. Only surface roles read it; none is a rectangle. Hit testing uses the same shape (pointed_face_contains).

   .. cpp:member:: bool point_leading

      The nose is at the left edge instead, its tip at the middle of the left edge: a right-to-left breadcrumb segment's (Control:: right_to_left). Read only with ``point``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<PartProfile> profile

      The part's profile beside its rectangle (PartProfile), or none. Only surface roles read it; a pointed face ignores it.

   .. cpp:member:: PartFrame frame

      Whether the part draws its frame. A surface part with frame none is its material's ground: the material's border and per-edge borders are left out, and its fills, keylines, shadows and shape are kept. It is the field of a borderless TextBox (Windows Forms BorderStyle.None), whose composite (a ComboBox, an up-down, the grid's cell editor) draws the frame itself. A primitive that draws no theme material has no ground apart from its frame, so a frameless part it would draw is drawn by the built-in recipe instead. A pointed face with frame none draws no nose edges. Roles that are not surfaces ignore it. Default: ``{PartFrame::drawn}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<AccentIndex> accent

      The part's position in the theme's accent ramp (§8.5), or none. A surface part with an accent is tinted from the ramp over its material; text and mark roles ignore it.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<Color> content_color

      The colour the part shows as its content (design language 8.5; owner decision W-7), such as a colour picker's sample, or none. Only the fill role honours it: a fill with a content colour is painted that colour, flat, in the part's shape (corner_radius, else square), in place of the recipe's material; every other role ignores it. A swatch is therefore a fill inside a well part that frames it. The colour is data, as an image is: it is drawn as given in ordinary and high-contrast presentation alike (the framing well follows the high-contrast recipe, so the sample stays outlined), and when the part is disabled (T2 GIVEN E2-1: a theme styles only what surrounds it, so a disabled swatch shows through its well, edge and label).

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<Color> authored_background

      The colours an application authored for this part (design language 8.5; owner decision T4-1), or none, such as a DataGridView cell style's BackColor and ForeColor. They stand where an inline style's colours would stand for this one part: over the theme's ordinary declarations, under its important ones, and never in high contrast, where the role's colours stand (§5.8). Unlike the content colour they are a look, not data. - The background: a surface role (and an indicator or glyph without a mark, which paints its ground) paints it flat in place of the material's fills, keeping the material's border, keylines and shadows and the part's shape. A fill with a content colour shows the content colour. A disabled part keeps it. - The foreground: text roles and the caret draw in it, marks are inked with it and rules (keyline, divider, seam) are drawn in it. A disabled part keeps the theme's disabled colours, so disabled stays greyed; a theme text colour declared important stands over it for text, and a glyph colour declared important for marks. A rule that chooses a primitive for the part at an important level stands over both; a primitive that draws a theme material takes the background in its material's fills, and a primitive's own colour parameters do not read them.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<Color> authored_foreground

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<PartKey> key

      The part's identity across frames (T2 GIVEN H3-1), or none. A part with a key has a record in its window's engine part table; one without is drawn statically, with no transitions and no cached raster. It is identity, not vocabulary: it changes nothing a part looks like today. Its views are borrowed for the call.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string_view control_type

      The control type whose theme rules style the part (an anatomy control id, such as "v_scroll_bar"), when a control draws a part on behalf of a stock control it embeds (a scrollable control's built-in scroll bars, so that ``v-scroll-bar::part(thumb)`` styles them as it styles a VScrollBar); empty, the default, for the painting control's own type. Only the type selector changes: the painting control's id, classes, ancestors and inline style still match. Borrowed for the call.

   .. cpp:member:: Insets granted_overflow

      How far past ``bounds`` the control lets the part be drawn when a theme scales it (T2 GIVEN D5-1, E8), in logical units. The presentational scale grows the part about its centre only as far as this allows: with none granted, the default, a part never grows. Layout and the part's hit region ignore it.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<Size> measured_text

      The text's measurement in ``font``, when the caller has already made it (paint_text_part_at does), so the text is not measured again; none otherwise. A cache, not vocabulary: it changes nothing drawn, and a caller that sets it must have measured exactly ``text`` in ``font``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<PartArc> arc

      The arc and needle roles' angles (PartArc), or none, when neither draws. Every other role ignores them.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<PartLightPlanes> light_planes

      The baked light planes the part is composited from (PartLightPlanes), or none. Such a part is drawn as the bake's light, room · R + Σ colour · power · L_i toned (engine_light::composite_light_planes), stretched over ``bounds`` at the window's scale, in place of its recipe and of any primitive a rule would choose: the bake carries the part's whole look, its body included. The composite is transparent where the bake's coverage is 0, so its light never reaches past the part; a part with a corner radius is clipped to that shape as well. The recipe draws the part instead in forced high contrast, where the role's colours stand, and when no raster of the part's size can be made (ornament gives way first).

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PartProfile final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/part_painting/part_painting.hpp>``

   A part's profile beside its rectangle (look integration; the T4 rendering reference's tab stack and the owner's Plan Paint tab look), as a theme sets it on the part (ControlPartMetrics) and the control passes it on: - ``taper``: the trailing (right) edge leans in by this many units at the part's top, a file-folder tab's edge, drawn as a line in ``taper_colour`` when it has one; - ``flare``: the bottom corners flare outward by this many units in a concave lip of ``flare_colour``, where a selected tab meets the plane; - ``edge_shadow``: a soft shadow beyond the trailing edge, following its taper, falling off from ``edge_shadow_colour`` to clear over this many units. A tapered part is cut to its shape, its outer shadows with it; the edge shadow then draws its cast. Each is bounded: the taper by the part's width, the flare by its height, all by maximum_part_shape.

   .. cpp:member:: double taper

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<Color> taper_colour

   .. cpp:member:: double flare

   .. cpp:member:: Color flare_colour

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: PartRaster final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_raster/part_raster.hpp>``

   One part's pixels as the engine keeps them (T2 plan, PartRaster): premultiplied BGRA8 in device pixels, in PartRasterView's layout, made for one display scale. Rows are tight: row_bytes is width * 4. Lifetime: create() makes a raster with every pixel transparent; its maker fills it through view() and then shares it read-only, as std::shared_ptr<const PartRaster>, with every recorded command that composites it (Painter::draw_part_raster). Recorded commands keep it alive until their display chunk is rebuilt or retired.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: bool opaque() const noexcept

      Whether record_opacity found every pixel opaque since the last view().

   .. cpp:function:: BorrowedSpan<std::uint8_t> pixels() const& noexcept

      Borrowed from the raster: the sanitizer tree reports a use after the raster died (the last shared owner let it go).

   .. cpp:function:: DeviceExtent extent() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: std::size_t row_bytes() const noexcept

   .. cpp:function:: DeviceScale scale() const noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static std::shared_ptr<PartRaster> create(DeviceExtent extent, DeviceScale scale)

      A transparent raster of :cpp:func:`extent <gui_forms::PartRaster::extent>` for display scale :cpp:func:`scale <gui_forms::PartRaster::scale>`, or null when either side exceeds maximum_part_raster_side or the scale is not a finite value in [minimum_device_scale, maximum_device_scale]. An empty extent is allowed and has no pixels.

   .. cpp:function:: PartRasterView view() noexcept

      The raster as a destination for a primitive: valid while the raster lives and is not shared read-only. Clears the recorded opacity, since the pixels may change through the view.

   .. cpp:function:: void record_opacity() noexcept

      Records whether every pixel's alpha is 255. The maker calls it after its last write through view(), before sharing the raster; the composite then copies the raster's rows rather than blending them.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PointerOverlay final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/primitive_registry/primitive_registry.hpp>``

   How a primitive behind the overlay seam is composited (E9-A, owner 2026-10-22: the seam serves the sheen only). Its raster is ``size``, in logical units, baked once per (size, state, scale) and never per pointer position; the engine composites it with its centre across at the part-local pointer, its top at the part's top, clipped to the part's shape, and damages only the part's extent when the pointer moves within it. While the pointer is not over the part, and always under reduced motion, its centre rests at ``rest`` times the part's width. It follows the part's presentational scale as a face does: baked at the scaled size at rest, and stretched with the face while the scale moves.

   .. cpp:member:: Size size

   .. cpp:member:: double rest

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PresentationState final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   One state of a part's presentational state machine (E7; design language §9.6). The machine is in the first of its states whose ``when`` states the part's published states all include (an empty ``when`` always matches), and moves its property to ``target``. A part whose states match none rests at the property's own value: its cascade value, or 1 for opacity. When the machine enters this state from another, or the part first appears in it, ``enter_from``, if set, stands the property at that value first; this is how a part fades in when it appears (the docking tiles, T2 GIVEN C2-1). Otherwise every move starts from the value shown, so a transition retargets and never restarts. A state of another machine of the property (another rule's) is another state: a part arriving in this state from it enters it (T3 P7b, an animation in a rule of its own). ``stops`` (E9.6, multi-stop @keyframes), at most maximum_motion_stops, make the move into this state pass each stop's value at its offset of the duration, offsets strictly increasing within (0, 1), values within the property's range; they need a timed curve. They play when the move into the state begins: on entering it from another state, or on appearing in it from ``enter_from``. A part that appears in the state without an entry value stands at its target, as before. An animated parameter shows each stop at its nearest keyframe level, as any moving value (E7r). The machine is presentational only: it reads the published states and writes nothing back, so it can neither create an observable state nor delay behaviour. ``target_em`` and ``enter_from_em`` (T3 P8c, owner decision D4) are em coefficients, as each stop's value_em: the value is the number plus the part's em times its coefficient. Only a translation's and a number parameter's machine takes them.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<AnatomyState> when

   .. cpp:member:: double target

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<double> enter_from

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MotionStop> stops

   .. cpp:member:: double target_em

   .. cpp:member:: double enter_from_em

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PrimitiveCost final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/primitive_registry/primitive_registry.hpp>``

   A declared upper bound on a primitive's work. One rasterization costs at most ``per_device_pixel`` arithmetic operations for each destination pixel, plus ``fixed`` for the whole part. One preparation costs at most ``preparation_per_device_pixel`` for each device pixel of the part's box; it runs once per part geometry, not once per frame. Budgets are checked against these at load (E4).

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t per_device_pixel

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t fixed

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t preparation_per_device_pixel

An ``animated`` parameter is one a transition drives from frame to frame, such as the bulge's amplitude during the ribbon's rise. .. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PrimitivePreparation final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/primitive_registry/primitive_registry.hpp>``

   What a primitive derives once from a part's geometry and fixed parameters, and reuses for every rasterization in which only animated parameters change. The dithered bulge, for example, derives its tone's full-amplitude extremes here, so that the rise does not recompute them each frame. The caller owns a preparation and keeps it beside the part's raster (in E2, the part's record in the window's EnginePartTable). It records what it was derived from: the primitive, the part's logical size and scale, the read states it saw, and every parameter value. A rasterization whose primitive, size, scale, read states or non-animated parameters differ is refused as stale; the caller prepares again. A preparation borrows nothing: ``primitive`` is compared, never called through.

   .. cpp:member:: const EnginePrimitive* primitive

      The primitive that derived the values; null until the first successful prepare().

   .. cpp:member:: Size logical_size

   .. cpp:member:: DeviceScale scale

   .. cpp:member:: AnatomyStateSet states_read

      The target's states restricted to the descriptor's states_read.

   .. cpp:member:: PrimitiveParameterBlock parameters

      The parameters as they were at preparation. Only non-animated values are compared.

   .. cpp:member:: std::array<double, maximum_prepared_values> values

      ``count`` live values, as many as the descriptor's prepared_values; what each means is the primitive's own business.

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t count

Otherwise, when either axis is sliced, the reference is the target at ``reference_size`` with ``reference_extent``; when neither is, the part is baked whole and counted as a fallback.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: ProceduralFill final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/procedural_fill/procedural_fill.hpp>``

   One procedural fill's parameters. The pattern is generated per display scale from these alone, in integer arithmetic, so that its bytes are the same on every host; each scale is generated, never resampled from another. - ``seed`` chooses the noise; equal parameters give equal bytes. - ``tile`` is the pattern's repeat in logical units: the side of its square tile, round(tile × scale) device pixels, within [2, 256]; a pinstripe's period. The fresco covers the box and does not read it. - ``strength`` in [0, 1] scales how far the figure departs from the ground; for the fresco, its blooms' opacity. - The ground is the pattern's base colour, and what the fill draws, as a solid, until its pattern is ready; the figure is its contrasting colour (fibres, grain, stripes, streaks); the fresco also reads ``accent``, its gradient's last stop, and ``bloom``, whose alpha is its blooms' peak opacity.

   .. cpp:member:: ProceduralPattern pattern

      Default: ``{ProceduralPattern::felt}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t seed

   .. cpp:member:: double tile

      Default: ``{64.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double strength

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color ground

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(128, 128, 128)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color figure

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(96, 96, 96)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color accent

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(128, 128, 128)}``.

   .. cpp:member:: Color bloom

      Default: ``{Color::rgba(255, 255, 255, 0)}``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: PropertyTransition final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   A transition on one resolved property: ``opacity``, or a number parameter of the rule's primitive that the primitive declares animated (such as the bulge's amplitude), so that its frames never remake the part's preparation. It runs on the window's MotionClock with ``curve``; every duration is clamped to the 400 ms ceiling. Under reduced motion and in an occluded or minimized window it jumps to its end (GIVEN D2-1, D4-1).

   .. cpp:member:: std::string property

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<PresentationState> states

   .. cpp:member:: MotionCurve curve

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: RasterCacheStatistics final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

   The raster cache's counters, for instrumentation.

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t rasters

      Rasters held and their bytes now.

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t bytes

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t hits

      Requests answered from the cache, drawn into reused storage, drawn into new storage, and drawn without caching.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t storage_reuses

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t storage_allocations

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t uncached

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t evictions

      Rasters evicted to keep within the budget.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t bakes

      Every time a primitive drew pixels: a whole part or a slice reference. Compositing a cached raster, or assembling one from a cached reference, is not a bake.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t slice_bakes

      Slicing (E7r): references baked; parts assembled from a reference already held; parts of a slicing primitive baked whole because neither axis could be sliced, and axes kept whole, each counted per draw.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t slice_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t slice_whole_fallbacks

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t slice_axis_fallbacks

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t keyframe_idle_bakes

      Keyframes (E7r; owner A, 2026-10-22): baked in idle frames ahead of use, baked on demand the first time a paint reached one not yet held, and taken from the shared bakes already held; and those queued for idle frames now.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t keyframe_demand_bakes

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t keyframe_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t idle_keyframes_queued

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t shared_evictions

      The shared bakes: evicted to keep within their budget, held now, and their bytes. The bytes, and the evictions, include image variants'.

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t shared_rasters

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t shared_bytes

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t image_variant_bakes

      Image faces' sized variants (owner, 2026-10-22): made from their images, lent from those held, and held now. Each is made once while it is held; a theme change or a change to the window's images drops them all.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t image_variant_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t image_variants

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t procedural_generations

      Procedural fills' patterns (E10): generated in idle frames, lent from those held, and asked for by a bake before they were ready (the fill then drew its ground); held now, and queued for idle frames now. Their bytes count with the shared bakes'. A theme change keeps them.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t procedural_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t procedural_placeholders

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t procedural_patterns

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t procedural_queued

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t backplane_bakes

      The window's backplane images (E9-B, owner 2026-10-22): made, lent from those held, and held now; those of them the window's renderer replayed whole (stage T5, C6); and the backplane commands their making skipped. Without a renderer that replays, the core bakes a backplane image from the window's backplane material and the backplane plane's engine part rasters alone (option 1), so text and other commands drawn in the backplane plane are absent from glass; each one is counted here every time an image is made over it. A replayed image skips nothing.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t backplane_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t backplane_images

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t backplane_replays

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t backplane_skipped_commands

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t image_mask_fallbacks

      Bakes whose image mask's variant was asked for and not lent (E9.6): the part, or the backplane region, was drawn unmasked, as the built-in path draws it.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t finish_tile_bakes

      World finish tiles (cp31): painted in idle frames, lent from those held, asked for before they were ready (nothing drawn there yet), evicted to keep within finish_tile_budget_bytes, and not held for want of room among tiles the latest paint used; held now, their bytes, and queued now.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t finish_tile_hits

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t finish_tile_misses

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t finish_tile_evictions

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint64_t finish_tile_refusals

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t finish_tiles

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t finish_tile_bytes

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t finish_tiles_queued

.. cpp:struct:: RecipeDeclaration final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   One recipe field's declared value. ``initial`` is CSS's ``initial``: the floor's value, and the declaration still wins over those beneath it; its value members are not read. Otherwise, by field: - text, outline_colour, muted_text, glyph, default_outline_colour and fill_text: ``colour``; - outline_style: ``outline_style``; - outline_width and default_outline_width (0 to maximum_recipe_outline_width) and outline_offset (within ±maximum_recipe_outline_offset): ``number``, in logical units; - text_shadow: ``shadowed``, false for none; its ``shadow_offset``, ``shadow_blur`` (the shadows' blur-radius convention) and ``colour``; - font_family: ``font_role``, and ``font_family`` for a numbered bundled family (FontSpec::family, zero for a role); font_style: ``italic``; - font_size (in logical units, with em_lengths in the control's own font size), font_weight and letter_spacing (logical units, with em_lengths in the resolved size): ``number``; - the canvas fields: fill_colour, stroke_colour and halo_colour ``colour``; stroke_dash, stroke_gap, halo_width, corner_radius, grid_spacing, grid_band and flow_spacing (logical units, em admitted), and flow_speed (world units a second) ``number``; flow_style ``flow_style``; texture_layers ``texture``.

      Default: ``{RecipeField::text}``.

.. cpp:class:: ResolvedControlRecipe final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/types/theme_types.hpp>``

   A control's recipe for one role and state (T3 P6f): the theme's built-in recipe (the floor, Theme::resolve(role, context), borrowed from the theme) or, when the theme's rules set a recipe field for the control (RecipeField), the floor's copy with those fields in place, which this value owns. The copy is made only then, so a theme whose rules set no recipe field costs nothing. A value for one paint or measurement: it borrows the theme, so it is not kept past the theme's use. ``recipe()`` borrows from the value, so it is read from a named value and never from a temporary.

.. cpp:struct:: RuleAncestor final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   One ancestor compound of a selector (selector step 4): a control type, a stable ID and classes, each optional, matched against an ancestor control. ``combinator`` relates it to the compound on its right: the subject's for the nearest, else the next nearer ancestor compound's. Ancestor compounds name no state, family, part, role or surface, so a rule's ancestors change only when an ancestor's type, ID or classes do, or a control moves.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: RuleDeclaration final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   One declaration: a property and its value. Every property but :cpp:func:`primitive <gui_forms::RuleDeclaration::primitive>` is a parameter of the primitive the same rule selects, so a rule that sets parameters names its primitive too. An expression's program reads inputs by position (engine_input_position, published_value_position), states, and the primitive's number parameters by their declaration position. A parameter it reads has the value the cascade gives it before any expression, so expressions never read one another. ``number`` is the expression's fallback. Its value is clamped to the parameter's range.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static RuleDeclaration primitive(std::string_view primitive_name)

   .. cpp:function:: static RuleDeclaration number_value(std::string_view parameter, double value)

      A choice parameter's value by the choice's name, such as a shape.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string property

   .. cpp:member:: RuleValueKind kind

      Default: ``{RuleValueKind::number}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string name

   .. cpp:member:: double number

   .. cpp:member:: Color colour

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ExpressionInstruction> program

   .. cpp:member:: ColourOrigin origin

      A colour parameter's origin (EC4).

   .. cpp:member:: double number_em

      A number parameter's em coefficient (T3 P8c): its value is ``number`` plus the part's em times it, held within the parameter's range when the part is painted. The rules check ``number`` against the range only where this is 0.

.. cpp:struct:: RuleSelector final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   ``family``, when set, is a control visual family's registered name (the theme's ControlVisualRole, such as ``accent_button``): the selector matches only parts resolved for that family (PartFacts::family). ``excluded`` holds the arguments of its ``:not()`` selectors: it matches no part any of them matches. ``custom_states`` (R2-13) are the custom states its ``:state()`` names: each a position in its control type's ControlAnatomy::custom_states, or, when its part is an item part, in the custom states of the item system declaring that part. A position means something only for one control type, so a selector with custom states names its control type (an inline style's rules, which apply to their own control alone, need not). They match the part's AnatomyStateSet custom bits.

A ``:not()`` adds its most specific argument's, and each ancestor compound its own.

.. cpp:struct:: StrokeStyle final

   ``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_marks/paint_marks.hpp>``

   A stroke's colour, width, joins, caps and dashes, in the painter's current coordinates. ``dash_count`` of the ``dashes`` are live, alternately on and off lengths; 0 (or an odd count, which is refused as solid) draws solid. ``dash_phase`` shifts the pattern's start along the stroke (an effect moving it on the motion clock animates the dashes, piece C13).

   .. cpp:member:: Color color

   .. cpp:member:: double width

      Default: ``{1.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: StrokeJoin join

      Default: ``{StrokeJoin::miter}``.

   .. cpp:member:: double miter_limit

      Default: ``{4.0}``.

   .. cpp:member:: StrokeCap cap

      Default: ``{StrokeCap::butt}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::array<double, maximum_dash_intervals> dashes

   .. cpp:member:: std::size_t dash_count

   .. cpp:member:: double dash_phase

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: SurfaceMaterial final

   ``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/types/surface_material_types.hpp>``

   SurfaceMaterial is a bounded ordered fill stack plus optional border, bounded shadow stack, and shared corner radius.


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static SurfaceMaterial from_parts(const MaterialFillLayer* fills, std::size_t fill_count, const MaterialShadow* shadows, std::size_t shadow_count, const MaterialBorder* border, double corner_radius)

   .. cpp:function:: static SurfaceMaterial from_parts(const MaterialFillLayer* fills, std::size_t fill_count, const MaterialShadow* shadows, std::size_t shadow_count, const MaterialBorder* border, const MaterialBorderEdges* border_edges, double corner_radius)

   .. cpp:function:: static SurfaceMaterial from_parts(const MaterialFillLayer* fills, std::size_t fill_count, const MaterialShadow* shadows, std::size_t shadow_count, const MaterialBorder* border, const MaterialBorderEdges* border_edges, const MaterialKeyline* keylines, std::size_t keyline_count, double corner_radius)

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_fill_layers

      Default: ``8U``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_shadows

      Default: ``4U``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_keylines

      Default: ``8U``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_glows

      Default: ``2U``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr double maximum_glow_reach

      The farthest a glow reaches outside the box, in logical units: the engine material's granted overflow on every side. Default: ``8.0``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr double maximum_filter_reach

      The farthest the part filters reach outside the box, in logical units: the drop shadow's larger offset component plus 1.5 × its blur, plus 1.5 × the filter blur. The engine material's outsets hold it. Default: ``8.0``.

   .. cpp:member:: constexpr double maximum_reflection_reach

      The farthest a reflection reaches outside the box, gap and reach together, in logical units (T5-Q15: the blur and glow cap). Default: ``8.0``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialFillLayer> fills

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialShadow> shadows

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialGlow> glows

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<MaterialBorder> border

   .. cpp:member:: MaterialBorderEdges border_edges

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialKeyline> keylines

   .. cpp:member:: double corner_radius

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<MaterialCornerRadii> corner_radii

      Corners that differ (E9.6): when present they replace corner_radius in the engine's material primitive. The built-in path, paint_surface_material, rounds every corner by corner_radius, so a theme sets that to the radius the fallback should draw.

   .. cpp:member:: MaterialColourFilter colour_filter

      The part filters (E9.6, CSS ``filter``), applied to the baked raster in this order: the colour filter to each pixel, then the drop shadow beneath, then the blur over the whole. A theme's filter list lowers to that order. The engine's material primitive draws them; the built-in path, paint_surface_material, draws none of them, and the window's backplane image draws only the colour filter.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<MaterialDropShadow> drop_shadow

   .. cpp:member:: double filter_blur

      CSS ``filter: blur(σ)`` as filter_blur = 2σ logical units, the blur radius convention of shadows; 0 for none.

   .. cpp:member:: double finish

      The surface finish (E9.6, CSS coverage row 9), from 0, the material as authored, to 1, a full gloss derived from its first fill (finished_surface_material, which both paths draw). A finish above 0 needs a solid or linear first fill and room for one more keyline.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<MaterialFillLayer> mask

      An alpha mask (E9.6, CSS ``mask-image``): a solid, gradient or image fill whose alpha, times its opacity for an image, multiplies the whole finished part, its shadows and glows included; outside the box it extends at its edge value, so that a shadow fades with the face beside it. The engine's material primitive draws it; the built-in path, paint_surface_material, draws the material unmasked.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<MaterialReflection> reflection

      A reflection of the finished face (MaterialReflection), drawn after the mask; none by default.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: Theme final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme/theme.hpp>``

   Theme is an immutable validated role/state recipe table plus structural spacing, geometry, typography, and motion tokens; it resolves logical visual context without exposing renderer or platform objects.


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: std::string_view id() const& noexcept

      Returns the immutable theme identity.

   .. cpp:function:: const BasicControlStyle& basic_style() const noexcept

      Returns the compatibility color projection used by explicitly legacy-styled controls.

   .. cpp:function:: const ThemeStructureTokens& structure() const noexcept

      Returns immutable spacing, geometry, typography, and motion token scales.

   .. cpp:function:: const ThemeDefinition& definition() const noexcept

      Returns the complete immutable definition for inspection or derivation.

   .. cpp:function:: const ThemeEngine& engine() const noexcept

      The theme's engine, which paint_part calls for every part. The theme owns it, so every window showing the theme shares it (T2 plan).


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: static std::shared_ptr<const Theme> create(const ThemeDefinition& definition)

      Validates bounded identity, every ordinary/selected/high-contrast recipe, and ordered finite structural tokens before publishing shared immutable ownership.

   .. cpp:function:: const ControlVisualRecipe& resolve(ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context) const noexcept

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedControlRecipe resolve(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context) const

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedControlRecipe resolve(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context, AnatomyStateSet states) const

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedControlRecipe resolve(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context, AnatomyStateSet states, std::string_view part) const

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedControlRecipe resolve(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context, AnatomyStateSet states, std::string_view part, std::string_view control_type) const

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedControlRecipe resolve(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context, AnatomyStateSet states, std::string_view part, std::string_view control_type, const ThemeItem& item) const

      Bounds role/state indices and chooses ordinary, selected, high-contrast, or high-contrast-selected recipe deterministically.

   .. cpp:function:: void provenance(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole role, ControlVisualContext context, CompiledThemeRules::PartProvenance& result) const

      Where each field of ``control``'s recipe in ``role`` and ``context`` came from (T4 cascade spec, item 5), for an editor's query: the facts resolve() reads, through CompiledThemeRules::resolve_provenance, into ``result``, its parameters left unset (a recipe has no primitive). In forced high contrast the fields the engine's pass draws are at forced_high_contrast in its layer, unauthored. Paint never calls it; the borrows in ``result`` stay valid while this theme, the control's inline style and its window's user theme live.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedFont resolve_font(const Control& control, FontSpec own) const noexcept

      ``control``'s primary text font (T4 fonts): ``own``, the font it holds, with the font fields (RecipeField::font_family to letter_spacing) of the theme's rules matching it at rest in place: no part, the region role, and the states of a default ControlVisualContext. Forced high contrast keeps them: they are not colours. An em size is of ``own``'s size, an em spacing of the resolved size. Where the fields make no font valid_font_spec admits, or a size outside minimum_rule_font_size to maximum_rule_font_size, it is ``own`` with no field themed.

   .. cpp:function:: ResolvedFont resolve_font(const Control& control, FontSpec own, std::string_view part, AnatomyStateSet states) const noexcept

      A named part's font in ``states`` (a tab label's, larger while selected): the control's own font with the font fields of the rules naming the part (and the control's own) in place.

   .. cpp:function:: bool declares_clarity(const Control& control) const noexcept

      Whether the rules matching ``control`` at rest (its type, classes and stable ID, no part or role, the window's media and the control's user theme and inline style) declare it clear (owner decision 2026-11-04): ``background: transparent``, a background colour of alpha 0 and no visible background layer, so that the surface's backdrop shows through it. A colour or layer left to the floor is not clear.

   .. cpp:function:: std::optional<BackdropChain> window_backdrop_chain(const Window& window) const noexcept

      The backdrop chain the rules selecting ``window`` declare (``window { backdrop: ... }``, owner decisions 2026-11-05): the winning ``backdrop:`` among the theme's and the user theme's rules, matched against the window's media and its root control's classes and stable ID, lowered operation by operation. A role colour in tint() is the colour the matching rules give it under the window's scheme. Nullopt where no rule declares one or the winner is ``none``. ``popup`` rules are not read here: they wait for native popup windows (stage T5).

   .. cpp:function:: BandDeclaration window_band(const Window& window) const noexcept

      The band the rules selecting ``window`` declare (``window { band-width: ...; band-color: ...; band-lit-color: ...; band-effect: ... }``; owner, 2026-11-08): each field from the last matching rule that sets it, among the theme's and the user theme's rules, matched as window_backdrop_chain matches. Empty where no rule sets one.

   .. cpp:function:: bool supports(ColourScheme scheme) const noexcept

      Whether the theme has a variant for ``scheme`` (T4 theme variants): its colour_schemes, which its sheet's ``color-scheme`` sets.

   .. cpp:function:: Color accent(AccentIndex index) const noexcept

      The ramp colour for an accent index; an index past the end of the ramp wraps around it.

   .. cpp:function:: const std::vector<MaterialShadow>& overlay_shadow(bool high_contrast) const noexcept

      The overlay shadow's layers (owner decision RB-37) for ordinary or high-contrast presentation; empty when the theme casts none.

   .. cpp:function:: std::optional<ImageId> icon_override(std::string_view identifier) const noexcept

      The image a compiled user theme gives the program icon ``identifier`` (its ``icons/<identifier>.png``), held by the theme while it lives; nullopt where it gives none (T3 user themes). Whether the program lets the icon be replaced is the application's (ApplicationResources:: icon).

   .. cpp:function:: std::optional<ThemeRoleColours> application_role(std::string_view name, const MediaFacts& facts) const noexcept

      The colours of the application colour role ``name`` (lowercase, as the sheet's ``color-roles`` declares it) where ``facts`` hold: its last declaration whose condition holds. Under forced colours with no ``forced-colors`` declaration of its own, its fallback role's high-contrast pair. Nullopt where the theme declares no such role.

With gui_forms, ``controls`` names the stock control types too: the light sheet's rules for a type outside it are warnings that drop them, named for ``gui-forms/light.css``. Default: ``{default_theme_base}``.
