.. cpp:struct:: ThemeDefinition final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/types/theme_types.hpp>``

   ThemeDefinition is one named, complete compatibility palette, role/state material matrix, and structural-token set.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string id

      Default: ``{"windows-professional"}``.

   .. cpp:member:: BasicControlStyle compatibility

   .. cpp:member:: ControlRoleTable roles

   .. cpp:member:: ThemeStructureTokens structure

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<Color> accent_ramp

      At least one colour; Theme::create refuses an empty ramp. Default: ``{provisional_accent_ramp()}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialShadow> overlay_shadow

      The overlay shadow's layers, outer (not inset) and at most SurfaceMaterial::maximum_shadows; empty casts none. Default: ``{provisional_overlay_shadow()}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialShadow> high_contrast_overlay_shadow

      The overlay shadow in high contrast, under the same rules. Empty by default: high contrast draws no cast shadow, and the overlay's high-contrast edge separates it from the content below.

   .. cpp:member:: ThemeRuleSheet rules

      The theme's rules (the cascade, T2 E4): which primitive draws which part. Empty by default: every part keeps the built-in recipe. They are compiled when the theme is made; an invalid rule or declaration is dropped with a diagnostic (Theme::engine().rules().diagnostics()).

   .. cpp:member:: ThemeRuleSet rule_set

      Whether the rules are a program's theme or a user theme's (T3 user themes): a user theme's rules are at the user levels only, and its other fields are unused while it is layered under a program's. Default: ``{ThemeRuleSet::program}``.

   .. cpp:member:: ThemeWindowBackdrop window_backdrop

      The backdrop request and backplane bases (stage T5). Empty by default: an opaque window, drawn as before.

   .. cpp:member:: ThemeDarkVariant dark_variant

      The dark variant (stage T4 phase 5). None by default. Default: ``{ThemeDarkVariant::none}``.

   .. cpp:member:: ColourSchemeSet colour_schemes

      The colour schemes the theme has variants for (T4 theme variants): light alone by default. A window in a scheme its theme lacks shows GUI.Forms' own theme in that scheme (Window::theme_fallback). A sheet declaring ``color-scheme`` (rules.colour_schemes) replaces it when the theme is made.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:class:: ThemeEngine final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_engine/theme_engine.hpp>``

   The theme engine's entry point (T2 plan, ThemeEngine). paint_part hands it every part; controls never call it directly. Ownership: a Theme owns its engine, and every window showing the theme shares it. An application's windows share one theme (ApplicationResources), so they share one engine. The engine is immutable once its theme is made: it holds the theme's compiled rules (the cascade, E4), below them the engine's own built-in rules (built_in_theme_rules, E7), and borrows the built-in primitive registry. What changes from frame to frame belongs to each window's EnginePartTable and MotionClock. The engine draws a part with a primitive only when the cascade selects a registered primitive for the part, its parameters validate, the cost is within budget (GIVEN F1-1) and the presentation is not forced high contrast. In every other case the built-in recipe draws it: the body paint_part had before the engine existed. A theme with no rules of its own draws every part with the built-in recipe except those the built-in rules address: the ribbon's command faces and group buttons, which take the dithered bulge and its rise, and the docking tiles, which fade in. A keyed part's transitions run on its window's motion clock (E7): the part's states select each machine's state, the property moves toward it, and the part repaints within its extent as it moves. Its opacity multiplies a primitive's composite in the shared blend; a part the built-in recipe draws is drawn as one paint group (Painter::begin_group) composited once at that opacity. A part painted without a key stands at its machines' targets and its ambient effect's pose.


   .. rubric:: Construction

   .. cpp:function:: ThemeEngine()

      An engine with only the built-in rules.

   .. cpp:function:: explicit ThemeEngine(const ThemeRuleSheet& sheet)

      An engine with ``sheet`` compiled against the built-in registry. Invalid rules and declarations are dropped with diagnostics; nothing throws for them.

   .. cpp:function:: ThemeEngine(const ThemeRuleSheet& sheet, ThemeRuleSet set)

      An engine with ``sheet`` compiled as ``set``: a user theme's has no built-in rules and is consulted beside a program theme's (CompiledThemeRules::compile_user).


   .. rubric:: Properties

   .. cpp:function:: const PrimitiveRegistry& registry() const noexcept

      The primitives the engine's rules may select.

   .. cpp:function:: const CompiledThemeRules& rules() const noexcept


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: PartPlan plan(const Control& control, const PartPaint& part) const noexcept

      The cascade's decision for one part of ``control``, without painting.

   .. cpp:function:: void paint(Painter& painter, const Control& control, const PartPaint& part) const

      Paints one part of ``control`` (see paint_part). A part with a key (PartPaint::key) and paintable bounds, painted by a control attached to a window, has its record in that window's part table made or updated first; a primitive then draws into the record's cached raster. A part without a key leaves no record; a primitive draws it into a raster of its own for this paint. If the primitive refuses the part, the built-in recipe draws it. A part with a blend (blend_property) is drawn inside a paint group of that composite.

   .. cpp:function:: void paint_clipped(Painter& painter, const Control& control, const PartPaint& part, std::span<const Rect> clips) const

      As paint, with what it draws repeated under each of ``clips`` (each in a save, clip_rect and restore; none for an empty span), the part planned, noted and baked once (N91): a pointed part's body and the strips of its nose.

   .. cpp:function:: bool paint_material_blocks(Painter& painter, const Control& control, const MaterialBlocks& blocks) const

      Paints ``blocks.material`` repeated over ``blocks.area`` of ``control`` (the node canvas's texture layers; cp31), through the material primitive and the window's caches as a keyed part is: - The block is square, a whole number of periods across: as many as fit maximum_material_block_side device pixels at the window's scale, and at least one. The primitive bakes it once into the record ``blocks.key`` names, and again only when the material, the scale or the block changes; panning bakes nothing. - A procedural fill's pattern is generated in an idle frame, never here (E10): until then the block shows the fill's ground, and the window repaints the area once the pattern is made. - The raster is then drawn at every block of the lattice that meets the area, in one save, clip_rect and restore. Draws nothing and returns false for a control in no window, an empty or non-finite area or anchor, a period outside its range, no material, a material the primitive refuses (its bake over the material bound) or no raster. Allocation failure propagates as std::bad_alloc, as paint's does. Not reentrant, as paint is not.

   .. cpp:function:: bool paint_finish_tiles(Painter& painter, const Control& control, const FinishTiles& tiles) const

      Paints ``tiles.finish`` over ``tiles.area`` of ``control`` from the window's world finish tiles (EnginePartTable::finish_tile; cp31): - The finish is evaluated at world device pixels, at the window's scale times the zoom, on the lattice of finish_tile_side-pixel tiles through world (0, 0) (snapped to the nearest device pixel, so tiles land on whole pixels). Each tile is the kernel at those pixels, so adjacent tiles join exactly: no period, and no seam. - A tile not held is queued for an idle frame and its place left bare; once the queue is painted the window repaints the area (the record ``tiles.key`` names). Panning reuses the tiles held; a new zoom or scale names new tiles, painted again in idle frames. - Cost: one draw_part_raster per tile meeting the area, inside one save, clip_rect and restore; nothing is painted inside the paint. Draws nothing and returns false for a control in no window, an empty or non-finite area, anchor or zoom, or no finish; otherwise returns whether every tile was held. Not reentrant, as paint is not.

   .. cpp:function:: PartHitRegion hit_region(const Control& control, const PartPaint& part) const noexcept

      The hit region of one part of ``control`` (E8, T1's hand-off): the part's bounds and the shape its primitive draws there, as plan() decides it. A part the built-in recipe draws, for any reason, has a rectangle. Themes choose a primitive's shape; they never set a hit region (design language §7.4 rule 1). Test a point with part_hit.

   .. cpp:function:: Rect text_slot(const Control& control, const PartPaint& part) const noexcept

      Where one part of ``control`` lays out its text (E8, T1's hand-off): the part's bounds less its primitive's content insets at the window's scale and text scale, as plan() decides it. Where the insets leave no room across or down, the slot shrinks to the bounds' centre line on that axis, so it never leaves the part. A part the built-in recipe draws has its bounds. Like the hit region, it ignores the presentational scale.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: ThemeMotionTokens final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/types/theme_types.hpp>``

   ThemeMotionTokens defines quick, standard, emphasized, and busy-cycle durations without prescribing an animation engine.

   .. cpp:member:: std::chrono::milliseconds quick

      Default: ``{100}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::chrono::milliseconds standard

      Default: ``{180}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::chrono::milliseconds emphasized

      Default: ``{280}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::chrono::milliseconds busy_cycle

      Default: ``{1400}``.

A resource of another kind cannot be a user theme's file; it is left out and named in ``omitted``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: ThemeRule final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   .. cpp:member:: std::string label

   .. cpp:member:: CascadeLevel level

      Default: ``{CascadeLevel::theme_sheet}``.

   .. cpp:member:: RuleSelector selector

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<RuleSpecificity> specificity

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<RuleDeclaration> declarations

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<EngineVersion> requires_engine

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<PropertyTransition> transitions

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<AmbientEffect> ambient

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<MaterialDeclaration> materials

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<RecipeDeclaration> recipe_fields

   .. cpp:member:: MediaCondition media

      The windows the rule holds in (EC3); by default, all.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ScopedDefinition> scoped_definitions

      Scoped custom properties (P12): the definitions the rule declares, and the conditions under which the rule, a copy, applies.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ScopedCondition> scoped_conditions

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<std::string> reads

      Inline custom properties (P12b). A theme's rule lists the custom properties it reads, through any number of definitions: where a control's inline definition of one wins for a part, the rule is passed over there and the inline style's copy of it applies instead. ``source_position`` is the position of the style rule the rule was lowered from in the theme's source (its syntax node, from 1), which places such a copy among the theme's rules; 0 for none.

   .. cpp:member:: std::uint32_t source_position

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<BackdropDeclaration> backdrop

      A surface rule's backdrop (``backdrop:``), or none.

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<BandDeclaration> band

      A window rule's band (``band-*``), or none.

   .. cpp:member:: RuleProvenance provenance

      Where the rule was written. Not compared: two rules that cascade alike are equal wherever they were written.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: ThemeRuleSheet final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/theme_rules/theme_rules.hpp>``

   A theme's rules, as authored, with the engine version the theme declares it needs and the values an application publishes that its expressions read (E6; Window::publish_value). The name at position n of the list is read at published_value_position(n). Each is 1 to 64 characters of lowercase ASCII letters, digits, '-' and '_', the first a letter, and is listed once; at most maximum_published_values.

   .. cpp:member:: EngineVersion requires_engine

      Default: ``{current_engine_version}``.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ThemeRule> rules

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<std::string> published_values

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<ColourSchemeSet> colour_schemes

      The colour schemes the sheet declares (``:root { color-scheme: ... }``, T4 theme variants), or none where it declares none. Theme::create takes a declared set as the theme's (ThemeDefinition::colour_schemes).

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ApplicationColourRole> application_roles

      The application colour roles the sheet declares (``color-roles``), in declaration order; at most maximum_application_colour_roles.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: ThemeWindowBackdrop final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/types/theme_types.hpp>``

   The theme's window backdrop (stage T5, owner decision T5-Q8, option 1): what a window showing the theme asks the compositor for, and the base its backplane is drawn from over each backdrop the window may be granted (notes:planning/stages/T5-compositor/PLAN.md §4.1).


   .. rubric:: Methods

   .. cpp:function:: const SurfaceMaterial* base_for(WindowBackdrop backdrop) const noexcept

      The base for ``backdrop``, or null when the theme gives none.

   .. cpp:member:: WindowBackdropRequest request

      The request a window makes while its application has made none (Window::request_backdrop). Value-initialized: ``opaque`` alone, so a theme that says nothing asks for nothing. Theme::create refuses a request window_backdrop_request_valid refuses.

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ThemeBackdropBase> bases

      At most one base per non-opaque backdrop, in any order; held out of line, so the definition stays small. Over a granted non-opaque backdrop the window's backplane is drawn from that backdrop's base, typically a partly clear tint over a material, or clear around a drawn shape over ``transparent``. Without one it is drawn from the window recipe's material, as over ``opaque``, which has no base.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: UnkeptPartAssembly final

   ``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

   One of a window's assemblies for parts that keep none of their own (a part without a record, and every part's derived dark variant): the assembly, the role whose dark variant darkened it (none for a light assembly) and whether it holds one yet.

   .. cpp:member:: AssembledPartMaterial assembled

   .. cpp:member:: std::optional<ControlVisualRole> darkened

   .. cpp:member:: bool filled

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

.. cpp:struct:: UserThemeFiles final

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme_compiler/theme_compiler.hpp>``

   A user theme's files (T3 user themes, notes:planning/stages/ T3-theme-language/USER_THEMES.md): its name, the stem of its main file ``themes/<name>.css``; that file's bytes; and the files of its folder ``themes/<name>/``, each by its path inside the folder, as the host read them. The folder's top-level ``.css`` files are the theme's sub-sheets, its top-level ``.png`` files its image resources (``url("resource:wood.png")``), and ``icons/<identifier>.png`` its icon overrides; nothing else is admitted.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string name

   .. cpp:member:: std::string main_sheet

   .. cpp:member:: std::vector<ThemePackageFile> files

   .. cpp:member:: bool header_implied

      A sheet named on the command line or in GUI_FORMS_THEME_SHEET is a theme by being named: its ``@theme`` header is implied (no engine bound, version or author), and one it has is read as usual.

   ``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme_compiler/theme_compiler.hpp>``

   The header of a user theme's main file, which makes the file a theme.

   .. cpp:member:: bool present

      Whether the main file opens with one: a file without it is no theme.

   .. cpp:member:: std::string version

   .. cpp:member:: std::string author

   .. cpp:member:: std::string provenance

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/authored_gradient/authored_gradient.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_authored_gradient(const AuthoredGradient& gradient) noexcept

   Whether ``gradient`` is one a layer can draw: a gradient kind; one to maximum_authored_gradient_stops stops; finite numbers; radii not negative; a circle's radius a length, without a fraction; a conic stop without units.

.. cpp:function:: bool authored_gradient_fixed(const AuthoredGradient& gradient) noexcept

   Whether a valid ``gradient`` resolves to the same layer for every box: no corner and no units anywhere, no circle (whose radius is the same in both directions, so a different fraction of each), and, for a repeating linear gradient, stops that run from 0 to 1 once placed. Such a layer is in normalized coordinates or a CSS angle.

.. cpp:function:: void resolve_authored_gradient(const AuthoredGradient& gradient, Size box, MaterialFillLayer& layer)

   Writes a valid ``gradient``, for a part's box ``box`` in logical units, into ``layer``, which is reset first: its kind, geometry, spread and stops, the other fields at MaterialFillLayer's defaults. A fixed gradient (authored_gradient_fixed) ignores ``box``. The layer: - places the stops as CSS does: a first stop without a position at 0, a last one at 1, a stop before an earlier one at the earlier one, the others evenly between their neighbours; - for a gradient that does not repeat, keeps the stops within the drawn range and adds the colours at its ends, interpolated as the engine interpolates (straight colour, rounded to 8 bits: at most half a step from the exact colour); - for a repeating one, scales its geometry to the span from the first stop to the last; a radial one's stops are rotated to begin at its centre. A span of 0 draws CSS's average colour, a solid; - separates coincident stops (CSS's hard stops) by 2^-20 of the gradient, which the layer's validity requires: under a tenth of a device pixel on a gradient shorter than 100,000 device pixels; - is a solid for a single stop.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_authored_gradient_stops

   The most stops one gradient may have: resolving adds at most two, the colours at the ends of a clipped or rotated stop list. Value: ``maximum_gradient_stops - 2U``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect/effect.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: template <std::size_t Capacity> constexpr BoundedRange<Capacity> bounded_range(std::size_t count) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: constexpr std::uint64_t effect_default_seed(std::string_view stable_id, std::string_view effect_name) noexcept

   The default seed of an effect on a control: FNV-1a over the control's stable ID, a separator and the effect's name, so runs, tests and motion checksums reproduce. An application may set its own (effect_seed).

.. cpp:function:: constexpr double effect_fraction(double x) noexcept

   x minus the largest integer not above it, for x strictly between -2^52 and 2^52; 0 otherwise.

.. cpp:function:: constexpr double effect_clamp(double x, double low, double high) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: constexpr double effect_mix(double a, double b, double t) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: constexpr double effect_smoothstep(double edge0, double edge1, double x) noexcept

   0 below edge0, 1 above edge1, u²(3 − 2u) between; equal edges give 0.

.. cpp:function:: inline double effect_sine(double x) noexcept

   The sine of x radians (engine_sine_cosine: the same bits on every host).

.. cpp:function:: constexpr Color effect_faded(Color color, double factor) noexcept

   A colour with its alpha scaled by ``factor``, clamped to [0, 1].

.. cpp:function:: constexpr std::string_view effect_time_model_name(EffectTimeModel model) noexcept

   The names the control schema and the effect_input property spell these with.

.. cpp:function:: constexpr std::string_view effect_input_name(EffectInputKind input) noexcept

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t effect_ticks_per_second

   The fixed tick every effect's time advances by: 1/60 s (owner decision 4(a)). Ticks are counted from the engine clock, never the wall clock. Value: ``60U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t maximum_effect_ticks_per_frame

   The most ticks one frame advances (owner decision 2(a)); a longer stall skips the rest, and the skip is counted. Value: ``4U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_effect_input_events

   The most input events one frame carries; the rest are counted, not kept. Value: ``8U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_effect_gradient_stops

   The most stops an effect's gradient holds. Value: ``6U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_effect_state_bytes

   The largest state any effect may declare, in bytes. Value: ``16384U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t current_effect_vocabulary_version

   The drawing vocabulary's version, which an effect names in EffectDeclaration::engine_version: 1 is the design note's first list (fills, strokes, gradients, soft dots and streaks); 2 adds the batched marks (polylines with joins, caps and dashes on a phase, polygons, bands, rectangle batches, segments, markers), the conic gradient and the checkerboard. A registry refuses an effect that names a later version. Value: ``2U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_effect_batch

   The most elements one batched vocabulary call draws (EffectPoints, EffectRects, EffectSegments); each call's declared fixed cost covers this many. Value: ``32U``.

``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_canvas/effect_canvas.hpp>``

   The deepest the canvas's save stack goes; a deeper save is refused. Value: ``4U``.

.. cpp:type:: EffectPoints = EffectArray<Point, maximum_effect_batch>

   The arrays a batched call draws: effect state, so fixed capacity.

.. cpp:type:: EffectRects = EffectArray<Rect, maximum_effect_batch>

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/effect_registry/effect_registry.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: template <typename Effect> requires EffectProgram<Effect> constexpr EffectEntry make_effect_entry() noexcept

   The generated table entry of one effect type. Its state must fit the budget it declares, and that budget the toolkit's maximum.

.. cpp:function:: bool effect_name_valid(std::string_view name) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: std::uint32_t effect_backdrop_half(double margin, double scale) noexcept

   The half width of the box passes for a backdrop blur reaching ``margin`` logical units at display ``scale``: floor(margin * scale / 3) device pixels.

.. cpp:function:: const EffectRegistry& built_in_effect_registry()

   The registry of the toolkit's own effects, filled on first use (defined with the controls that host them).

.. cpp:function:: void install_effect_registry(const EffectRegistry* registry) noexcept

   The process's installed effect registry: the one a theme compiled without a registry of its own checks ``effect`` names against (E417), such as the user theme a process is started with (--theme, GUI_FORMS_THEME_SHEET), settled at its first window. gui_forms_core holds only the address and never names a registry: the installer owns the registry, which must stay alive until it is uninstalled (null) or the process ends, and the core reads it when a held request is settled, not when it is held. None is installed until a program installs one; with none, ``effect`` names are checked for their form only and E417 is skipped, which the request's report says. Replacing or uninstalling does not reach a compile already done. Any thread; settling reads it on the UI thread.

.. cpp:function:: const EffectRegistry* installed_effect_registry() noexcept

.. cpp:function:: void install_built_in_effect_registry()

   Installs built_in_effect_registry() as the process's registry (install_effect_registry), which lives until the process ends (defined with the controls, so only a program linking them can call it). A program using the stock controls calls it once while it sets up, before its first window. Repeating it changes nothing.

.. cpp:function:: bool effect_verification_recorded(std::string_view name, std::size_t state_size, const void* run_thunk) noexcept

   Whether the effect pass of the gui_forms Clang plugin verified the effect named ``name``, of ``state_size`` bytes, whose run thunk is ``run_thunk``: a record naming it with that state size is in the binary holding that code (design note §2). A record of another size (another build's effect of the same name) does not count.

.. cpp:function:: EffectLoadDiagnostics effect_load_diagnostics() noexcept

.. cpp:function:: std::string_view effect_selection_source_name(EffectSelectionSource source) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: std::string_view effect_selection_outcome_name(EffectSelectionOutcome outcome) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: EffectSelectionOutcome check_effect_selection(const EffectRegistry& registry, std::string_view name, EffectSelectionSource source, std::string_view parent, Size part, double scale, const EffectEntry*& entry) noexcept

   The load checks for the effect ``name``, asked for by ``source``, on a part of a control whose effect parent is ``parent`` (EffectView<P>::parent_name), ``part`` logical units in size at display ``scale``. Sets ``entry`` to the registered entry when the outcome is ``selected``, else to null. An empty name is ``none``. Never fails.

.. cpp:function:: void note_effect_refused(const EffectSelection& selection) noexcept

   Counts one refused selection (an outcome other than ``selected`` and ``none``) in effect_load_diagnostics; the first in the process also writes one line to stderr naming the effect, who asked and why. A control calls it once per change of its selection, not once per paint. Any thread.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_effect_name_length

   Whether ``name`` may name an effect: snake_case (lowercase ASCII letters, digits and single '_'s, the first a letter, the last not '_'), at most maximum_effect_name_length characters, and not effect_none_name's ``none``, which themes write for no effect. Value: ``64U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_registered_effects

   The most effect types one registry holds. Value: ``64U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_effect_outset

   The farthest an effect may draw outside its part, in logical units. Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_effect_backdrop_margin

   The farthest an effect's backdrop blur reaches (EffectDeclaration:: backdrop_margin), in logical units: glass's own bound. Value: ``8.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t effect_backdrop_operations_per_pixel

   What sampling the backdrop costs a frame, per device pixel of the grown backplane image (EP-7): its copy into the blur's storage, six box passes of an add and a subtract per lane, and the copy out. Added to the effect's frame estimate when it is selected (check_effect_selection). Value: ``16U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint64_t maximum_effect_operations_per_frame

   The most operations one effect frame may cost on its part: the bound an animated primitive's part has (maximum_animated_operations_per_frame, GIVEN F1-1). Value: ``4'000'000U``.

An input that is not published, such as the pointer of a part that is not hot, reads as NaN, so an expression reading it gives its fallback. Value: ``32``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/engine_math/engine_math.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: double engine_logarithm(double x) noexcept

   Natural logarithm of ``x``. Requires x > 0 and finite; returns NaN for x < 0 or NaN, −infinity for 0, and +infinity for +infinity.

.. cpp:function:: double engine_exponential(double x) noexcept

   e raised to ``x``. Returns 0 below about −745, +infinity above about 709.8, and NaN for NaN.

.. cpp:function:: double engine_power(double base, double exponent) noexcept

   ``base`` raised to ``exponent``, for base ≥ 0. Exact cases: base^0 is 1, 1^exponent is 1, and 0^exponent is 0 for exponent > 0. Returns NaN for a negative or NaN base and for a NaN exponent.

.. cpp:function:: EngineSineCosine engine_sine_cosine(double x) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: double engine_arctangent2(double y, double x) noexcept

   The angle of the point (x, y) from the positive x axis, in radians in [−π, π], as std::atan2(y, x) (E9.6's conic gradient). Exact cases: (0, 0) gives 0, and a point on an axis gives 0, ±π/2 or π. NaN in either argument gives NaN. The ratio of the smaller to the larger magnitude is reduced twice (by π/4 and by the half-angle formula) to at most 0.2, where a 13-term series converges past 2^-60.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms::engine_light

``#include <gui_forms/engine/light_planes/light_planes.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: LampColour lamp_colour_from_srgb(Color colour) noexcept

   A colour given as sRGB, such as a control's lamp colour property, as a lamp's linear colour at unit power: each channel decoded by the sRGB curve (through engine_power, so every host decodes alike). Alpha is ignored.

.. cpp:function:: LightPlanesDefect read_light_planes(std::span<const std::byte> bytes, LightPlanes& planes)

   Reads the study's bake file (lamp_keys.cpp, write_buffers): three little-endian int32 (width, height, lamp count), then the room plane, each lamp's plane and the coverage plane as little-endian IEEE binary32. The whole buffer is validated before ``planes`` is replaced; on a defect ``planes`` is left as it was. Throws std::bad_alloc when the planes cannot be stored (``planes`` left as it was).

.. cpp:function:: std::size_t composite_light_planes(const LightPlanes& up, const LightPlanes& down, std::span<const LampDrive> drives, const LightComposite& settings, LightPlaneRect rect, const PartRasterView& destination) noexcept

   Composites ``up`` and ``down`` (the same bake for a key that is not pressed) with one drive per lamp over ``rect`` in ``destination`` (premultiplied BGRA8), writing every pixel of ``rect`` inside the destination: the key's pixels with their coverage as alpha, transparent where it covers nothing. The planes are sampled bilinearly in linear, premultiplied light, so a 240-pixel bake shown 180 units wide (the owner's choice) is filtered before it is toned; a rectangle the bake's own size reads each plane pixel exactly. Returns the pixels written; none when the two bakes' shapes or lamp counts differ, ``drives`` holds fewer than the lamp count, or ``rect`` is empty.

.. cpp:function:: double lamp_power(bool on, double seconds, bool reduced_motion) noexcept

   A lamp's power ``seconds`` after it was switched: 0 when off, the strike envelope when on (1 from lamp_strike_seconds, or at once under reduced motion). A lamp switched off while lit cools instead (lamp_cooling_power).

.. cpp:function:: double lamp_cooling_power(double from, double seconds, bool reduced_motion) noexcept

   A lamp's power ``seconds`` after it was switched off at power ``from``: from · (1 − u)², u = seconds / lamp_cool_seconds, and 0 from lamp_cool_seconds on, or at once under reduced motion.

.. cpp:function:: double key_press_mix(double from, double to, double seconds, bool reduced_motion) noexcept

   A key's press mix ``seconds`` after its press or release began from mix ``from`` toward ``to`` (0 up, 1 down): eased, reaching ``to`` at key_press_seconds, or at once under reduced motion.

.. cpp:function:: bool lamp_settled(double seconds) noexcept

   Whether a lamp switched on ``seconds`` ago and a press begun ``seconds`` ago have settled: the frames after it change nothing.

.. cpp:function:: bool key_press_settled(double seconds) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: bool lamp_cooled(double seconds) noexcept

   Whether a lamp switched off ``seconds`` ago has cooled: it is fully off.

.. cpp:function:: double lit_key_lean_degrees(double height_share) noexcept

   The sides' lean in degrees for a key ``height_share`` of its short side tall (lamp_keys.cpp, shape_key).

.. cpp:function:: bool light_plane_name_valid(std::string_view name) noexcept

   Whether ``name`` names a bake: 1 to 64 lower-case ASCII letters, digits and '_' (the baker's stems, such as dialog_ok_up), so that a name never leaves its directory.

``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_clock/motion_clock.hpp>``

.. cpp:var:: constexpr FrameInterval motion_frame_interval

   The clock's frame cadence while something moves. Value: ``std::chrono::milliseconds(16)``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/motion_value/motion_value.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: double motion_easing(MotionEasing easing, double progress) noexcept

   The eased progress for ``progress`` in [0, 1] (clamped), for tests and for later engine transitions. cubic_bezier and steps take their defaults here: the linear curve and one step; motion_curve_easing reads a curve's own.

.. cpp:function:: double motion_curve_easing(const MotionCurve& curve, double progress) noexcept

   The eased progress of a timed ``curve`` (its easing with its control points or steps) for ``progress`` in [0, 1] (clamped).

.. cpp:var:: constexpr FrameInterval motion_duration_ceiling

   The ceiling on every duration (design language §9.2; the 400 ms starting token). A longer duration or time constant is clamped to it. Value: ``std::chrono::milliseconds(400)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr FrameInterval follow_time_constant

   The exponential approach's time constant (GIVEN D3-1, "a fixed fraction of the remaining gap per about 75 ms"): each 75 ms the gap shrinks by a factor of e, so 63 % of what remains is covered. Value: ``std::chrono::milliseconds(75)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_motion_overshoot

   The largest overshoot a spring or the overshoot easing shows, as a fraction of the distance travelled (the 8 % starting token). A larger request is clamped to it. Value: ``0.08``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double motion_settle_tolerance

   The settled conditions' tolerance: half a device pixel at the window's scale. A settling value whose remaining gap (and, for a spring, whose movement over one 60 Hz frame) is within it snaps to its target. Value: ``0.5``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double motion_settle_frame_seconds

   Value: ``1.0 / 60.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_motion_extent

   The bounds of a value's extent (MotionValue::set_extent). Value: ``1e-6``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_motion_extent

   Value: ``1e6``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t maximum_motion_steps

   The most levels steps() takes. Value: ``32U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_motion_stops

   The most stops one move passes. Value: ``4U``.

.. cpp:type:: MotionListener = Delegate<const MotionStep&>

   A value's listener: a named member or function of the owner, bound with Delegate::bind. It runs on the UI thread, from the clock's frame or from the window event that ended the motion, after the value moved and after the owner was invalidated as declared. It may retarget, finish or jump any value, its own included; a value it starts is first advanced on the next frame.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_shape/part_shape.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: PartCornerRadii part_corner_radii(PartShape shape, Rect box) noexcept

   The radii of ``shape``'s corners drawn in ``box``, clamped to half its shorter side. A circle reports half the shorter side at every corner, though it is the inscribed circle, not a rounded box.

.. cpp:function:: double shape_signed_distance(PartShape shape, Rect box, Point point) noexcept

   The signed distance from ``point`` to the edge of ``shape`` drawn in ``box``, in the box's units: negative inside, positive outside.

.. cpp:function:: bool shape_contains(PartShape shape, Rect box, Point point) noexcept

   Whether ``point`` lies inside ``shape`` drawn in ``box``, all in the same units. The box's left and top edges are inside, its right and bottom edges outside.

.. cpp:function:: double shape_coverage(PartShape shape, Rect box, double scale, std::int64_t x, std::int64_t y) noexcept

   How much of the device pixel (x, y) ``shape`` covers when drawn in ``box`` (logical units) at ``scale`` device pixels per unit, from 0 to 1: one half minus the signed distance of the pixel's centre in device pixels, clamped. The same inputs give the same coverage on every host.

.. cpp:function:: bool part_hit(const PartHitRegion& region, Point point) noexcept

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t part_shape_kind_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(PartShapeKind::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::array<std::string_view, part_shape_kind_count> part_shape_names

   The shapes' registered names, in PartShapeKind order: the choices of a primitive's ``shape`` parameter.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_hit_target

   The pointer target minimum of dense chrome (design language §7.4, 20 × 20 logical units, proposed). Value: ``20.0``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/engine/part_table/engine_part_table.hpp>``

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t unkept_part_assembly_count

   How many assemblies a window keeps for such parts: a strip's tabs share a few identities (resting, selected, hot), so a few entries keep a repeated paint from assembling, or allocating, again. Value: ``8U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_item_part_records

   The most item-part records one window keeps before it evicts the least recently painted (see EnginePartTable). A record is about 800 bytes on 64-bit hosts (its preparation is about half of that); with its slot, its lookup text and its index entry it takes about 1 KB, so the cap costs at most about 4 MB per window. A part a primitive draws also has its CachedPartRaster (about 600 bytes, and a copy of its material when a material primitive draws it), two more copies of the material when the theme's rules set its fields (AssembledPartMaterial), and its raster, which the raster cache's budget bounds. The cap covers far more item parts than a window shows at once: a record evicted while its part is still on screen is only made again on the next paint. Each eviction is O(1). Value: ``4096``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t raster_cache_budget_bytes

   The most bytes of cached rasters one window keeps (GIVEN F2-1: 8 MB). Value: ``8U * 1024U * 1024U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t shared_bake_budget_bytes

   The most bytes of shared bakes one window keeps (E7r), apart from raster_cache_budget_bytes: slice references, a few kilobytes each for a button at scale 2, and keyframes, ten whole rasters for each size and state of a part with an animated parameter (some 200 KB for a large ribbon button at scale 1.5). A proposed default. Value: ``4U * 1024U * 1024U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_idle_keyframes

   The most keyframes queued for idle frames at once (E7r): ten levels for each of about six parts. A keyframe that does not fit is baked on demand when a paint first reaches it. Value: ``64U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_queued_patterns

   The most procedural patterns queued for idle frames at once (E10). A pattern that does not fit is queued again when a paint next asks for it. Value: ``32U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t finish_tile_side

   World finish tiles (cp31; the node canvas's finish texture): squares of finish_tile_side device pixels of a surface finish evaluated at world device pixels (engine_primitives::paint_world_finish), so that held tiles join with no seam. A tile is named by its finish, the display scale its raster is drawn at, the kernel's scale (device pixels per world unit: the display scale times the canvas's zoom), and its column and row on the lattice of tiles through the world's origin: tile (c, r) covers world device pixels [c * side, (c + 1) * side) by [r * side, (r + 1) * side). A paint takes the tiles held and queues the rest; idle frames paint them (EnginePartTable::generate_finish_tiles). Value: ``128U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t finish_tile_budget_bytes

   The most bytes of finish tiles one window keeps: 512 tiles of 64 KiB, a 2900-pixel square of one layer. The least recently used tile is evicted first, never one the latest paint used: a tile that finds no room is not held (counted as a refusal) and its place stays bare. A proposed default. Value: ``32U * 1024U * 1024U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_queued_finish_tiles

   The most finish tiles queued for idle frames at once; a tile that does not fit is queued again when a paint next asks for it. Value: ``256U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint64_t finish_tile_idle_effort

   The effort one idle frame spends painting finish tiles, in the material bake's effort units (finish_effort_weight per device pixel): four orange-peel tiles, one of hammertone. At least one tile is painted whatever its cost. Value: ``16'000'000U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_finish_tile_waiters

   The most parts waiting for finish tiles at once; a part beyond it is repainted when the window next repaints it. Value: ``32U``.

Value: ``256.0``.

.. cpp:function:: void material_declaration_lengths(const MaterialDeclaration& declaration, std::vector<double>& lengths)

   The lengths of ``declaration`` in logical units, into ``lengths`` (cleared first), in a fixed order: its number where it is a length (a border width, a corner's radius that is not relative, the filter's blur); each authored gradient's centre, radii and stop positions (their units); the image positions' and the image sizes' units; each shadow's offset, blur and spread; each glow's blur and spread; each keyline's width and inset; the drop shadow's offset and blur; the reflection's gap and reach.

.. cpp:function:: void set_material_declaration_lengths(MaterialDeclaration& declaration, std::span<const double> lengths) noexcept

   Sets the lengths of ``declaration`` from ``lengths``, in that order; a length past the end of ``lengths`` is kept.

   Adds ``em`` times each of the declaration's em_lengths to its length.

.. cpp:function:: void material_declaration_colours(const MaterialDeclaration& declaration, std::vector<Color>& colours)

   The colours of ``declaration``, into ``colours`` (cleared first), in a fixed order: its colour; each authored gradient's stop colours; each shadow's, glow's and keyline's colour; the drop shadow's.

.. cpp:function:: void set_material_declaration_colours(MaterialDeclaration& declaration, std::span<const Color> colours) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: void seat_shadows(double height, Color colour, std::vector<MaterialShadow>& shadows)

   Appends the seat's shadows for a part ``height`` logical units above its panel (negative: below it; resolved, not a share) in ``colour`` to ``shadows``: the cast shadow (a raised part's contact shadow, a sunk part's lip shade) then the crevice, each while ``shadows`` holds fewer than SurfaceMaterial::maximum_shadows; a seat with no room is dropped (ornament gives way first). A height of 0 appends nothing.

.. cpp:function:: void assemble_material(const SurfaceMaterial& floor, const std::array<const MaterialDeclaration*, material_field_count>& fields, Size box, MaterialAssemblyContext context, SurfaceMaterial& material)

.. cpp:function:: const ThemeRuleSheet& built_in_theme_rules()

   The engine's built-in rules (E7), compiled below every theme's: the docking drop tiles' fade (T2 GIVEN C2-1) and the ribbon's dithered rise (D3-1, G4-1). Static storage, made once on first use.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_rule_ancestors

   At most this many ancestor compounds in one selector, and ancestors are matched within this depth of the subject: a control nested deeper than maximum_ancestor_depth below an ancestor is not matched against it. Value: ``8U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_ancestor_depth

   Value: ``256U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view primitive_property

   The name of the declaration that selects a part's primitive. Its value is a registered primitive's name, or ``built_in`` for the built-in recipe. Value: ``"primitive"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view built_in_primitive_name

   Value: ``"built_in"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t colour_role_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(ColourRole::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_application_colour_roles

   Application colour roles (owner request 2026-11-09, the product ports): roles a theme package declares beside the built-in ones, for colours its program means, such as ``syntax-keyword``. A sheet declares them in an unconditional ``:root`` block, :root { color-roles: syntax-keyword from ink-on-paper, ... } each with the built-in role it falls back to, which also gives its meaning: a pair or a single colour, and the colour forced high contrast shows for it unless the sheet declares a ``forced-colors`` variant. Its colours are then declared like a built-in role's, in ``:root`` and under ``@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`` or ``(forced-colors: active)``; a role without an unconditional declaration of its own starts from its fallback's declarations, its own after them. Rules read it with role(), and program code through Theme::application_role or Control::theme_role_colours. Names are 1 to 64 characters of lowercase ASCII letters, digits and '-', the first a letter, none a built-in role's; at most maximum_application_colour_roles in one theme. Value: ``32U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_application_colour_role_name

   Value: ``64U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_backdrop_blur

   Value: ``32.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_backdrop_saturation

   Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_theme_band_width

   The widest ``band-width`` a theme may declare, in logical units (the same as gui_forms::maximum_band_width, which a window checks). Value: ``32.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view opacity_property

   The engine's presentational property (T2 GIVEN C1-1): one multiplier, from 0 to 1, on the part's whole composite. It is no vocabulary atom and no primitive parameter; only a transition sets it, and a part without one is opaque. Value: ``"opacity"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view scale_property

   The engine's presentational scale (T2 GIVEN D5-1, E8): the part drawn at this multiple of its size about its centre, within the overflow its control grants (PartPaint::granted_overflow), its hit region and layout unchanged. Like opacity it is no atom and no parameter; only a transition sets it, and a part without one is drawn at 1. It is a capability: the built-in rules never use it, and a part granted no overflow never grows. Value: ``"scale"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_presentational_scale

   Value: ``0.5``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_presentational_scale

   Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view face_property

   The engine's face cross-fade (E7r form a): when a primitive-drawn part's face changes between discrete states (its raster is replaced), the old face fades into the new over the transition's curve, each baked once and blended through a paint group (old at 1 - t, new at t, added). The transition names no states: it runs on every change of face. A change while a fade runs fades from the face shown last to the new one. The two faces' rasters must have the same pixel extent and display scale: a new face baked at another size (such as a part resting at a popped size) replaces the old one without a fade. The presentational scale does not stop a fade: while it moves, both faces are stretched over the same rectangle (E7r form b). Like scale, no built-in rule uses it. Value: ``"face"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view translate_x_property

   The engine's presentational translation (E9.6, CSS ``translate``): the part drawn moved by this many logical units across or down, within the overflow its control grants, its hit region, layout and text slot unchanged. The part's raster is composited at the offset, so a move bakes nothing. Like scale it is no atom and no parameter; only a transition sets it, a part without one rests at 0, and a part granted no overflow (beyond what its scale uses) never moves. A machine's values lie within ±maximum_presentational_translate; the grant clamps further. Value: ``"translate_x"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view translate_y_property

   Value: ``"translate_y"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_presentational_translate

   Value: ``32.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view blend_property

   The engine's blend property (E9.6): a declaration of this name, whose value is the name ``normal``, ``multiply`` or ``screen``, draws the part through a paint group of that composite (PaintGroupComposite), whatever draws it, so that it multiplies or screens what was painted beneath it. Like opacity it is no atom and no primitive parameter, and a rule that declares it needs no primitive; the last matching rule that declares it wins. The group's bounds are the part's extent (its bounds grown by its primitive's outsets and its granted overflow): nothing the part draws beyond them is shown. The forced high-contrast pass draws no blend. Value: ``"blend"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view effect_property

   The effect a part draws (effect programs, design note section 7): a declaration of this property whose value is a registered effect's name (snake_case, as EffectDeclaration::name spells it) selects that effect for the part, and the name ``none`` (effect_none_name) selects none. Like blend it is no atom and no primitive parameter, and a rule that declares it needs no primitive; the last matching rule that declares it wins. The rules check only the name's form: the control hosting the part checks the effect against its registry when it attaches it (the effect's parent type, its budget and its verification record), and a refused effect leaves the part to its primitive alone (EffectSelectionOutcome). Value: ``"effect"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view effect_none_name

   Value: ``"none"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view effect_primary_property

   The colours a part's effect draws with (EP-4, theme-variants direction): a primary and a secondary colour role, never a literal, so that an effect follows the theme's palette in each colour scheme (the rule copies the palette's media variants make) and the forced high-contrast pass remaps it by its role. A declaration of either holds a colour whose origin is a role (ColourSource::role); the last matching rule that declares each wins. Like effect_property, neither needs a primitive. A part whose rules declare none takes its host control's own colours. Value: ``"effect_primary"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view effect_secondary_property

   Value: ``"effect_secondary"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t text_role_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(TextRole::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view font_property

   The engine's font property (T3 P8c, D6): a declaration of this name, whose value is a name (a text role's, or font_inherit_name), sets the part's text role. The last matching rule's wins; a part no rule sets has the ui role. It is recorded in the part's resolution, where it gives the part its em; controls' text does not read it yet. Value: ``"font"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view font_inherit_name

   Value: ``"inherit"``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_presentation_states

   The most states one presentational state machine has, and the most transitions one part runs: opacity, scale, face and both translations, with room for a parameter. Value: ``8``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_part_transitions

   Value: ``6``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_ambient_frames_per_second

   The ambient budget (design language §9.4, the accepted starting tokens): at most 30 frames per second, over a region of at most 10 % of the window on everyday surfaces. A surface the application marks ceremonial (Control::ceremonial, GIVEN B3-1) lifts the area cap for the parts on it. Value: ``30.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_ambient_window_fraction

   Value: ``0.10``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t material_field_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(MaterialField::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_seat_height

   -- the seat ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ A part seated in its panel (skeudoindustrial surfaces; the theme's "object presence"): a key, a well or a plate that stands proud of the panel, or sinks into it, by a height a theme sets per role (``seat``). Under the one fixed light from the upper left (the finishes' light), a raised part casts a thin contact shadow down and to the right and darkens the crevice all round where it meets the panel; a sunk part's lip shades its upper left inside, and its seam darkens. Both are outer or inset shadows of the material, so they are baked with it, identical in any repaint rectangle, and drawn by every path that draws shadows. The depth is a bas-relief's: at most maximum_seat_height, so a share of a long bar's shorter side stays a seat and the shadows stay within the material's outsets. Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr Color default_seat_colour

   The seat's shadow colour when the sheet gives none. Value: ``Color::rgba(0, 0, 0, 77)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double seat_light_run

   The run of the cast shadow per unit of height: the light stands 45 degrees above the panel, so the shadow falls half the height across and half down. Value: ``0.5``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t recipe_field_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(RecipeField::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_dash

   What the node canvas's part fields admit (RecipeField::fill_colour on): a dash or gap of 0 to 64 logical units (not both 0 unless both are, which is ``none``), a halo 0 to 16 wide, a corner radius 0 to 64, a grid spacing of 2 to 512 world units, a grid band starting at 2 to 128 view units, a texture contrast of 0 to 0.03 (the design language's sub-3 % texture rule: a declaration above it is refused, and the canvas clamps to it again when it paints), a flow speed of 0 to 512 world units a second and a flow spacing of 4 to 128. Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_halo_width

   Value: ``16.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_corner_radius

   Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_canvas_grid_spacing

   Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_grid_spacing

   Value: ``512.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_canvas_grid_band

   Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_grid_band

   Value: ``128.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_texture_contrast

   Value: ``0.03``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_texture_tile

   A texture layer's tile is 2 to 96 logical units, so that one tile with the material's outsets bakes within the material bound at scale 3 (maximum_material_block_side, ThemeEngine::paint_material_blocks); at most three layers. Value: ``96.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_canvas_texture_layers

   Value: ``3U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_flow_speed

   Value: ``512.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_canvas_flow_spacing

   Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_canvas_flow_spacing

   Value: ``128.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_recipe_outline_width

   What a recipe admits (valid_recipe): an outline at most 16 logical units wide, offset at most 64 either way; a text shadow offset at most 16 on each axis and blurred by at most 8. Value: ``16.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_recipe_outline_offset

   Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_recipe_stroke_width

   An arc or needle at most 32 logical units thick (stroke-width). Value: ``32.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_text_shadow_blur

   Value: ``8.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_text_shadow_offset

   Value: ``16.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_part_metric

   What a part metric admits (RecipeField::block_size to edge_shadow_width): a size at most 512 logical units, a margin within +-64, a gap from -64 to 512, a taper, flare or edge shadow at most 64. Value: ``512.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_part_margin

   Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_part_shape

   Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_stripe_pitch

   What a stripe pattern admits (RecipeField::stripe_pitch to stripe_speed): a pitch of 0 (auto) or 4 to 64 logical units, a pale share of 0.1 to 0.9, an angle of 15 to 75 degrees and a speed of 0 to 3 pitches per second. Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_stripe_pitch

   Value: ``64.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_stripe_ratio

   Value: ``0.1``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_stripe_ratio

   Value: ``0.9``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_stripe_angle

   Value: ``15.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_stripe_angle

   Value: ``75.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_stripe_speed

   Value: ``3.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_rule_font_size

   What a font field admits (T4 fonts): a size of 4 to 96 logical units once its em part is resolved, a weight of 100 to 900, and a letter spacing valid_font_spec admits at the size. Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_rule_font_size

   Value: ``96.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_rule_font_weight

   Value: ``100.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_rule_font_weight

   Value: ``900.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t custom_property_undefined

   One rule. ``label`` names it in diagnostics (T3: its source position). ``requires_engine``, when set, is the engine version the rule needs, in place of its sheet's. Its transitions and its ambient effect cascade as declarations do: for each property, the transition of the last matching rule that declares one (for a parameter, among the rules selecting the part's primitive), and the ambient effect of the last matching rule that declares one. Its material fields (EC1) cascade the same way, each on its own, from every matching rule, whatever primitive it selects: they change a part only where its primitive draws a theme material. Its recipe fields (RecipeField) cascade the same way for the control as a whole. ``specificity``, when set, is the rule's specificity in place of the one its selector gives (T3 engine change EC2): a rule expanded from CSS's ``:is()`` takes its most specific argument's. A class or stable-ID rule with a given specificity then needs a class or an ID in it rather than in its selector; an inline style still needs its selector's stable ID. Custom properties defined in more than one place (T3 P12, EC7; owner decision of 2026-10-29), resolved per part when the theme loads: each definition of such a name is a scoped definition of the rule that declares it, at the rule's level, selector and media; and a rule that reads such names is lowered once per combination of their candidate definitions, each copy holding the combination as its conditions. A part's winning definition of a name is the last matching one in ascending precedence, or none; a copy applies to a part only where every condition names the part's winner. A name defined once is substituted when the theme loads, and is not scoped. Names are written with their ``--``; a definition is identified by a number unique in the sheet. Value: ``0xFFFF'FFFFU``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_scoped_custom_properties

   The most scoped names one compilation's conditions may read (E515): eight times the token-heavy test theme's 8 (theme_custom_property_css_tests). Value: ``64U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_read_custom_properties

   The most custom property names a theme's rules read (P12b, ThemeRule::reads): a sheet defines at most 1,024. Value: ``1024U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t inline_definition_base

   A definition made in a control's inline style (P12b) is numbered at or above this, a theme's below it, so the two never share a number and the engine tells an inline definition's win apart. Value: ``0x8000'0000U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t maximum_operations_per_device_pixel

   Budgets (GIVEN F1-1): a part's primitive costs at most 512 declared operations per device pixel, and an animated part (one whose primitive has an animated parameter) at most 4 million per frame. A part beyond either keeps the built-in recipe; a primitive declaring no per-pixel cost cannot be bounded and is refused too. Value: ``512U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint64_t maximum_animated_operations_per_frame

   Value: ``4'000'000U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t animated_keyframe_levels

   Keyframes (E7r, owner 2026-10-22: 8 to 12). While an animated parameter's transition runs, the part shows the nearest of this many levels evenly spaced over the parameter's declared range, each baked once and shared; settled, it shows its exact value. When a transition starts, the levels it will pass are baked at once: at most this many bakes, each within the animated budget above, so that none is baked while it runs. Value: ``10U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_ambient_loop_frames

   The most frames in an ambient effect's loop (E7r): its time in the period steps through at most this many, or its frame rate's count for one period if fewer, each baked once, the first time it is shown, and shared. Value: ``24U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double user_theme_minimum_opacity

   The least opacity a user theme gives a part (USER_THEMES.md section 8, decision 7), so that no user theme hides a control outright. Value: ``0.5``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t sheet_diagnostic

   The rule position of a diagnostic about the sheet itself rather than one of its rules. Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(-1)``.

``#include <gui_forms/engine/types/engine_types.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: const char* raster_view_defect_name(RasterViewDefect defect) noexcept

   Registered (snake_case) name of a defect, for diagnostics.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr EngineVersion current_engine_version

   The version this build of the engine implements. E1 (the kernels, the registry and the bulge pair) is 0.1; E8 (the relief primitives, shapes and choice parameters) is 0.2; T3's EC1 (authored material fields and the material primitive's ``family`` recipe) is 0.3. Value: ``{0, 3}``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_device_scale

   The scales a primitive accepts. Below 0.5 the dithered lattice's dots would collide; above 8 no host reports a display. Value: ``0.5``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_device_scale

   Value: ``8.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::uint32_t maximum_part_raster_side

   The largest part raster, per side. Parts are small; the bound keeps every size product far inside std::size_t. Value: ``16384``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t part_raster_bytes_per_pixel

   Bytes in one pixel: blue, green, red, alpha in that memory order (BGRA8, the one pixel format of every surface and raster), each colour premultiplied by alpha (blue, green and red never exceed alpha). Value: ``4``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/painting/surface_material_painting.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_surface_material(const SurfaceMaterial& material) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: void paint_surface_material(Painter& painter, Rect bounds, const SurfaceMaterial& material)

.. cpp:function:: Insets surface_material_visual_outsets(const SurfaceMaterial& material) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: bool surface_material_opaque(const SurfaceMaterial& material) noexcept

   Whether paint_surface_material leaves the whole of its bounds opaque, whatever lay beneath: a valid, unfinished material with square corners, no visual outsets, filter or mask, and an opaque solid fill drawn normally, over which everything else it draws composites.

.. cpp:function:: bool surface_material_size_invariant(const SurfaceMaterial& material) noexcept

   Whether paint_surface_material draws each pixel of its bounds the same whatever the bounds' size: a valid, unfinished material with square corners, no border, shadow, visual outset, filter, mask or reflection, whose fills are all solid. Its pixels inside an old extent then stay right when the extent changes (Panel::paint_depends_on_size).

.. cpp:function:: std::optional<double> surface_material_resize_band(const SurfaceMaterial& material) noexcept

   How far in from the bounds' right and bottom edges a size change can alter what paint_surface_material draws, in logical units, when nothing else moves: 0 for a size-invariant material; for one whose only size-dependent parts are square or rounded corners and a solid border of one width on every edge, the larger of the corner radius and the border width. Empty for any other material (a gradient, an image, shadows, outsets, filters, masks, reflections, per-edge borders), which a size change alters anywhere.

.. cpp:function:: Insets box_shadow_visual_outsets(std::span<const MaterialShadow> shadows) noexcept

   How far the outer (not inset) shadows among ``shadows`` reach outside the rect that casts them: three blur radii plus any positive spread, shifted by each offset.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/surface_material/presets/material_presets.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: SurfaceMaterial gloss_material(const GlossColours& colours, double corner_radius)

   The gloss material (design language §4.3): one vertical four-stop gradient with a hard break near mid-face, a 1-unit shine keyline just under the top edge (inside the border, clipped to the corners), and a 1-unit border, with ``corner_radius`` in logical units. Throws std::invalid_argument for a corner radius that is not finite or lies outside [0, 4096].

.. cpp:function:: SurfaceMaterial finished_surface_material(const SurfaceMaterial& material)

   The material ``material.finish`` describes, with a finish of 0 (E9.6): the first fill's colours at 0, 0.5 and 1 along it (a solid fill's colour throughout) are rebuilt as the gloss's four vertical stops, keeping the fill's blend mode, its top half lifted and its break darkened, and a white shine keyline is added under the top edge, inside the border. A material whose finish is 0 is returned as it is. ``material`` must be valid (valid_surface_material); gloss_material is unchanged and is not finish 1 over its colours.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double gloss_break_start

   Where the gloss gradient breaks, as fractions of the face's height from its top: the colour steps from above_break to below_break between them. Value: ``0.48``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double gloss_break_end

   Value: ``0.52``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double finish_lift

   The surface finish's constants (E9.6, owner 2026-10-23), provisional until the owner's eye at the gate: at finish f, the face's top half is lifted towards white by finish_lift * f, the colour just below the break is darkened by finish_break_darkening * f, and the shine keyline's alpha is finish_shine_alpha * f. Value: ``0.25``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double finish_break_darkening

   Value: ``0.15``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double finish_shine_alpha

   Value: ``176.0``.

   The smallest and largest tile, in logical units. Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double maximum_procedural_tile

   Value: ``256.0``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/canvas_look/canvas_look.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: const ControlAnatomy& node_canvas_anatomy() noexcept

.. cpp:function:: CanvasLook resolve_canvas_look(const Theme& theme, const Control& canvas, ControlVisualContext context)

   Resolves every canvas part of ``canvas`` from ``theme`` (Theme::resolve with the part's name and node_canvas_control_id as the control type, so the rules match the canvas's classes and stable ID: the grid variants ``paper``, ``blue``, ``green`` and ``grey`` are classes). ``context`` carries the canvas's colour scheme and contrast (Control::visual_context); each part is resolved in its own states over it. - A field no rule sets takes the stock value the part table states, coloured from the theme's basic style. - Dark: where the theme derives its dark variant (ThemeDarkVariant:: derived), every colour is mapped by the dark colour rule by what it paints (the canvas as a well, nodes and ports as stock faces, headers as chrome, lines as lines, selection cues as cues, titles as ink, and the colours that carry state as signals, which keep their hue: a lamp on stays green, in error red, off neutral; an accepted drop green, a rejected one red; an invalid link red); a theme with an authored dark variant keeps its rules' colours and maps only the stock values. The texture screens instead of multiplying (CanvasTextureBlend). - Forced high contrast: no rule applies (the engine's pass); areas take the plate, lines the state's edge, a selected link a two-unit halo in the focus colour; minor grid lines and the marquee's fill are clear and there is no texture. Reads the theme only; the result owns everything it holds.

.. cpp:function:: CanvasLook resolve_canvas_look(const Theme& theme, const Control& canvas, ControlVisualContext context, const CanvasItem& item)

   The look of one item: as resolve_canvas_look above, with ``item``'s ID and classes matched by ``:item()`` rules, so every part an item draws (node, header, selection, port, port label, link, dashed link, link preview, lamp and focus ring) takes the rules written for it. A canvas paints the item's parts from this look and the canvas-wide parts (region, grid, marquee, knife, texture) from its own; it resolves one per distinct ID and class set it shows, on the same triggers, and keeps them. With no ID and no classes it is the canvas's look. Reads the theme only; runs the cascade on every call (Theme::resolve's item form keeps nothing).

.. cpp:function:: double clamp_canvas_texture_contrast(double contrast) noexcept

   The contrast a canvas texture is multiplied at: ``contrast`` clamped to [0, maximum_canvas_texture_contrast] (the design language's sub-3 % texture rule; the compiler already refuses more), 0 when not finite.

.. cpp:function:: ProceduralFill canvas_texture_fill(const CanvasTextureLayer& layer, CanvasTextureBlend blend) noexcept

   The procedural fill a texture layer bakes (canvas_texture_fill): the layer's pattern, seed, tile and strength, over the ground that leaves the canvas unchanged under the blend (white under multiply, black under screen), its figure the other extreme. So a pattern not yet generated (its ground drawn meanwhile, E10) changes nothing, and a generated one darkens or lightens by at most its strength times the layer's contrast.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_background(Painter& painter, const Control& canvas, const CanvasLook& look, Rect area, CanvasView view)

   The canvas background of ``canvas`` over ``area``: the region's colour, then each texture layer, then the grid (the level select_grid_level chooses for ``view.zoom``, one fill_grid_pattern pass with the level's fade). - A procedural layer is a baked pattern, never an image: a material of the one procedural fill canvas_texture_fill makes, baked by the theme engine (ThemeEngine::paint_material_blocks, record ``texture.<index>``). Its tiles are anchored at world (0, 0) and stay at the tile's logical size at every zoom, so the grain stays sharp. - A finish layer is the layer's finish, in its theme's colours, as a property of the world: evaluated at world device pixels at the window's scale times the zoom, from the window's world finish tiles (ThemeEngine::paint_finish_tiles, record ``texture.<index>``), so it is continuous everywhere, with no period and no seam between tiles, and pans and zooms with the canvas. - Each layer is drawn in one multiply or screen group, as the look's texture_blend says, at the layer's clamped contrast. - Cost: a procedural layer is baked once per change of the look or the window's scale, its pattern generated in an idle frame; a finish tile is painted in an idle frame, once per tile, scale and zoom, and kept while the window's finish tile budget allows (panning reuses them, a new zoom paints new ones; until then the place shows no finish). Each paint draws, per layer, one raster per block or tile meeting the area. A view that is not placed (a non-finite origin or zoom, or a zoom not above 0) draws the colour alone.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_node(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Rect body, double header_height, CanvasNodeState state)

   A node's body ``body`` with its header band, the top ``header_height`` view units (0 for none, clamped to the body), in ``state``: the body's area, the header's area clipped to the band and a line under it, then the body's outline over both.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_node_selection(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Rect body)

   The selection ring around a node's ``body``: the selection part's line, two view units outside the body plus half its width, with its corners.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_port(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Point centre, double radius, CanvasPortState state)

   A port: a disc of ``radius`` at ``centre`` in ``state``'s area.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_link(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, std::span<const Point> points, CanvasLinkState state, CanvasLinkStyle style)

   A link through ``points`` (2 or more, finite; a NaN breaks it as Painter::stroke_polyline does), as ``style``'s part in ``state``: its halo first, solid, then its stroke with round joins and caps and its dashes. Fewer than 2 points draw nothing.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_lamp(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Point centre, double radius, CanvasLampState state)

   A lamp: a disc of ``radius`` at ``centre`` in ``state``'s area.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_marquee(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Rect rect)

   The marquee over ``rect``: its area, then its outline with its dashes.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_focus(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Rect target, double target_radius)

   The keyboard focus ring around a focused node's body or port's disc ``target`` whose corners are ``target_radius`` (a port's radius for a disc): the focus part's line, canvas_focus_gap outside the target plus half its width, its corners the target's grown by the same reach plus the part's corner radius. Draws nothing for an empty target or a clear line; the width is never below minimum_canvas_focus_width.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_port_label(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, Point baseline, std::string_view text, FontSpec font, CanvasPortState state)

   A port's label ``text`` at ``baseline`` in ``font``, in the label ink of the port's ``state``. Draws nothing for empty text or a clear ink.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_knife(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, std::span<const Point> points)

   The knife stroke through ``points`` (as paint_canvas_link's), with its dashes. Fewer than 2 points draw nothing.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_canvas_link_preview(Painter& painter, const CanvasLook& look, std::span<const Point> points, CanvasPreviewState state)

   The link being dragged through ``points``, in ``state``: its halo, then its stroke with its dashes, as paint_canvas_link. Fewer than 2 points draw nothing.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::string_view node_canvas_control_id

   The node canvas's control id and anatomy (the part table in docs/user/theme-language.md, "node canvas"). The anatomy is the theme's, so the default theme's ``node-canvas::part(...)`` rules compile before a canvas control is registered: the theme compilers add it to the selector vocabulary when the controls they are given lack it. A canvas control returns it from Control::anatomy(). Static storage. Value: ``{"node_canvas"}``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t canvas_node_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(CanvasNodeState::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t canvas_port_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(CanvasPortState::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t canvas_link_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(CanvasLinkState::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t canvas_lamp_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(CanvasLampState::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t canvas_preview_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(CanvasPreviewState::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_canvas_focus_width

   The keyboard focus cue (the ``focus`` part) is a shape, not only a colour: a ring at least minimum_canvas_focus_width units wide, drawn canvas_focus_gap units outside the focused node or port, so it shows where a hue difference alone would not (forced high contrast, colour blindness, a theme whose focus colour is near its line colour). Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double canvas_focus_gap

   Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:function:: const ThemeDefinition& gui_forms_theme_definition()

   The floor: Plan Paint's action plate for push buttons, the gold active tool for selected ones, recessed wells for entry fields and choices, near-white stock (#F4F4F5) for windows and panels, flat command members at rest, and enabled text colours that meet the contrast gate (4.5:1) on every face (disabled text is exempt). Its recipes are the floor sheet's (gui_forms_floor_sheet), evaluated once on the first call (thread-safe) over the C++ structure (owner option 1) and kept for the process: the reference stays valid until exit (S3). Id ``gui-forms``; its rules are empty.

.. cpp:function:: Color item_content_text(const Control& control, ControlVisualRole family, AnatomyStateSet states) noexcept

   The text colour ``family``'s recipe gives ``control`` for an item in ``states``: the colour a control's default item content is drawn in, which it reports as ItemContentPaint::text.

.. cpp:function:: bool raise_item_content_paint(Event<ItemContentPaint&>& event, ItemContentPaint& item)

   Raises ``event`` once with ``item``, with ``item.painter`` saved and clipped to ``item.content`` and restored afterwards (also when a handler throws), so that nothing a handler draws reaches the plate, the focus cue or another item. Returns whether the control then draws its default content: true only when a handler set ``draw_default``. An empty or non-finite content rectangle raises nothing and returns false.
