   Registered (snake_case) name of a mark, as the schema's vocabulary lists it.

.. cpp:function:: BorrowedText lend_paint_text(std::string_view text)

   PartPaint::text is lent for one paint call (borrow sanitizer study, section 2.5, row 7). lend_paint_text stamps ``text`` from the innermost paint scope on the calling thread; the window opens one around each control's on_paint, so in the checked build a record (or a view taken from it) kept past that call fails at its next read, and so does one whose owner's text was replaced during the call by a nested scope's end. Outside any paint scope (a test or a bake painting a part directly) the text is not tracked. The ordinary build returns ``text`` unchanged.

.. cpp:function:: ControlVisualContext part_visual_context(const Control& control, AnatomyStateSet states) noexcept

   The theme context a part's states select: hot, pressed or captured, selection (selected, checked, current, open, active_tool), the visible focus cue and preselect. The control supplies what the part does not carry: its enabled, invalid and pending status, window activity and high contrast. A part that is itself disabled or unavailable is drawn disabled.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_part(Painter& painter, const Control& control, const PartPaint& part)

   Paints one part of ``control`` by its role (see PartPaint), through the engine of the control's effective theme. Roles that draw nothing of their own (region, edge, perimeter, sheen) are no-ops: the material of the surface they belong to draws them. An empty or non-finite ``bounds`` paints nothing. What disabled means (owner, 2026-10-20: "a change in font color or control highlighting. greyed out"): a disabled part greys the control's chrome, through the recipes' disabled states: the text colour of labels and captions, and the highlighting (edge, well, hot and selected plates). Content never changes: - the image role (T2 GIVEN H1-1) is a content picture, such as the docking stamp's snapshot, drawn exactly as given into ``bounds``, never tinted, dimmed or resized by a theme, disabled or not; - a content colour (GIVEN E2-1) is drawn exactly as given, disabled or not. An icon is chrome, the theme's or the command's symbol, and keeps its disabled treatment: 45 % opacity. The shadow role paints the theme's overlay shadow (owner decision RB-37): ``bounds`` is the rect of the surface that casts it (a tooltip's face, a menu's panel), with the corner radius of ``corner_radius`` or else of the ``family`` recipe, and the shadow falls outside it, down and right. Paint it before the surface. Only surfaces that really overlap content cast it; a control's own contact shadow stays in its material. Nothing is painted where the theme casts no overlay shadow (high contrast, by default). A control that paints a shadow part reports overlay_shadow_outsets from its visual_outsets, so that damage and replay cover the blur. A part without its own authored colours takes the control's (Control::set_back_color and set_fore_color): the control's own or inherited foreground, and its own background, or an inherited one on any role but a well.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_text_part_at(Painter& painter, const Control& control, PartPaint part, Point origin, double width)

   A text part (label, caption or content) placed by its baseline, as controls that lay out text by baseline draw it: ``part``'s bounds become its line box with the text starting at ``origin`` (a point on the baseline), at least ``width`` wide and never narrower than the text, and paint_part draws it. The line box is the one paint_part centres text in, so the text lands on ``origin`` exactly. A part without text draws nothing. A part that carries its text's measurement (PartPaint::measured_text) is not measured again.

.. cpp:function:: bool pointed_face_contains(Rect bounds, double point, Point position) noexcept

   Whether ``position`` lies in the pointed face of ``bounds`` whose nose is ``point`` units long (PartPaint::point): inside the bounds and left of the nose's edges. A point of zero or less is the whole rectangle.

.. cpp:function:: bool leading_pointed_face_contains(Rect bounds, double point, Point position) noexcept

   The same for a face whose nose is at its left edge (PartPaint:: point_leading): inside the bounds and right of the nose's edges.

.. cpp:function:: Insets overlay_shadow_outsets(const Control& control) noexcept

   How far ``control``'s theme's overlay shadow reaches outside the casting surface's rect, in the control's presentation (ordinary or high contrast). Zero on every side when the theme casts none.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_part_from_recipe(Painter& painter, const PartPaint& part, const ControlVisualRecipe& recipe, ControlVisualContext context)

   paint_part's drawing, with the recipe and context already resolved. For helpers that resolve a recipe themselves (the existing controls' focus and default cues), so that every part is drawn by one implementation. The shadow role paints nothing here: the overlay shadow is the theme's value, not the recipe's, so it is drawn only through paint_part.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t part_mark_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(PartMark::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::array<std::string_view, 9> part_value_names

   The values admitted to the vocabulary beside states and marks (design language 12.7), as the schema lists them: they have no enum, so this is the order they were admitted in.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/stock_anatomies/stock_anatomies.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: std::span<const ControlAnatomy> stock_control_anatomies() noexcept

   The anatomies of the stock controls (stage T4, phase 3): every control type of the control schema (``manifests/control-schema.json``), in schema order, generated into the core by ``tools/stock_anatomies.py`` so that a sheet the core compiles may name stock control types, parts and states without the controls library. Static storage: the spans stay valid for the life of the program (ControlAnatomy). An application compiling a theme package passes these, and its own controls' anatomies, as ThemeCompileOptions::controls.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme/resolved_font.hpp>``

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t font_field_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(FontField::count)``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme/theme.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: ThemeDefinition windows_professional_theme_definition()

   The Windows-familiar ``windows-professional`` theme's definition, a copy each call.

.. cpp:function:: std::shared_ptr<const Theme> default_theme()

   The built-in default theme: GUI.Forms' own, ``gui-forms`` (gui_forms_theme()), built once on the first call (thread-safe). It seeds ApplicationResources, a SurfaceRoot without resources and the C ABI's theme reset.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/theme_compiler/theme_compiler.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: ThemeCompileResult compile_theme_package(const ThemePackage& package, const ThemeLibrary& library, ImageRegistry& images, const ThemeCompileOptions& options)

   Compiles ``package`` with its ``@import`` closure among ``library``'s packages (one cascade: imported sheets first, and the later definition of a colour role or ``@keyframes`` wins) onto the built-in professional theme. Its image resources are loaded into ``images`` and owned by the compiled theme: each is removed from ``images`` when the last theme holding it is released, so replacing or dropping a theme removes its images. Themes compiled through one library that load the same bytes into one registry share one image. A refusal leaves none of its own. The package itself need not be installed. Compiles through one library, and the release of their themes, are not safe to run concurrently.

.. cpp:function:: ThemeCompileResult compile_theme_packages(std::span<const ThemePackage> packages, const ThemeLibrary& library, ImageRegistry& images, const ThemeCompileOptions& options)

   Compiles an application's theme (APP_WINDOW_RULES_DESIGN.md section A): ``options.base``, then ``packages`` in their order, each with its ``@import`` closure among ``library``'s packages, as one cascade, so a later package overrides an earlier one and may read its colour roles. A package another imports again keeps its earlier place. The theme is named after the last package, or is the base alone, under the base's id, when ``packages`` is empty. Every package is verified before any is compiled; two of one name, or more than maximum_theme_packages, are refused (E601), and the findings name each package's files. The combined sheets are bounded as one closure's are (1 MiB). Images are owned as compile_theme_package's are; compile_theme_package is the one-package case.

.. cpp:function:: ThemeValidation validate_theme_packages(std::span<const ThemePackage> packages, const ThemeLibrary& library, const ThemeCompileOptions& options)

   Checks ``packages`` as compile_theme_packages would compile them, keeping no theme and no image: for a test or a build step to catch a bad sheet before Application::create (gui_forms_add_theme_check in GUIFormsThemes.cmake runs it at build time). The images the sheets name are decoded into a registry of the call's own, released before it returns. Like compile_theme_package, not concurrently with another compilation through ``library``.

.. cpp:function:: std::string format_theme_diagnostic(const ThemeCompileDiagnostic& diagnostic)

   One finding as one line, ``<file>:<line>:<column>: <severity> <code> <name>: <detail>`` (the place left out where the finding has none, then the rule and property where it is inside one), as the build check and a test print it.

.. cpp:function:: std::shared_ptr<const Theme> default_compiled_theme()

   The process default theme: the theme a window shows while no application is current, the compiled base default_theme_base names alone. Built on the first call (thread-safe) and never recompiled; every call returns the same theme.

.. cpp:function:: ThemeCompileResult compile_user_theme(const UserThemeFiles& files, const ThemeLibrary& program, ImageRegistry& images, const ThemeCompileOptions& options, ThemeRuleSet set, UserThemeHeader* header = nullptr)

   Compiles a user theme as the package its files imply (USER_THEMES.md section 3): its sub-sheets in filename order (byte order of their names) and then its main file, one cascade; an ``@import`` of a sub-sheet (``@import "editor.css";``) places it there instead, and an ``@import`` of another name imports the program's package of that name from ``program``, pinned to the installed one. The main file opens with an ``@theme`` header (refused without, E601); its ``engine`` descriptor bounds the engine (E607). ``set`` chooses the levels: ThemeRuleSet::user for a theme layered under the program's (ApplicationResources::set_user_theme under the layered policy), ThemeRuleSet::program for one replacing it. Images are owned as compile_theme_package's are. ``header``, when given, receives the main file's header once it is read, whether or not the theme then compiles (present false where the main file has none or does not read).

.. cpp:function:: std::vector<ThemePackageExport> export_theme_packages(const ThemeLibrary& library)

   Every package of ``library``, in name order, as export writes it.

.. cpp:function:: ThemePackage theme_sheet_package(std::string_view sheet, std::string_view name)

   A theme package of one sheet: an application's theme as one plain CSS file, edited and read again at start-up without a rebuild. The manifest is made here, naming the package ``name`` and the sheet ``sheet.theme.css`` with its sha256 (gui-forms-theme/1, engine >=0.3, version 1.0.0). The name is checked when the package is compiled, as any manifest's is (E601); a quote, backslash or control character in it is escaped, so the manifest is always well-formed JSON.

.. cpp:function:: ThemeCompileResult compile_installed_theme(std::string_view name, const ThemeLibrary& library, ImageRegistry& images, const ThemeCompileOptions& options)

   As compile_theme_package, for the installed package named ``name``; an unknown name is refused (E603).

.. cpp:var:: constexpr ThemeBase default_theme_base

   The base of the process default theme (default_compiled_theme()), the one place that chooses it: gui-forms, as default_theme() (stage T4 phase 6, owner decision 2026-11-08). Value: ``ThemeBase::gui_forms``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_theme_packages

   The most packages compile_theme_packages takes: 16, as many as one package's ``@import`` closure may hold. Value: ``16U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_user_theme_files

   A user theme's limits (USER_THEMES.md section 2): its folder holds at most 256 files and 16 MiB. Its sheets, with the packages they import, are one closure under the package limits (at most 16 imports, 1 MiB). Value: ``256U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_user_theme_bytes

   Value: ``std::size_t{16} * 1024U * 1024U``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/theme/types/theme_types.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: std::string_view control_visual_role_name(ControlVisualRole role) noexcept

   Registered (snake_case) name of a recipe family, as the schema's vocabulary lists it; empty for ``count``.

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_control_visual_recipe(const ControlVisualRecipe& value) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_control_state_recipes(const ControlStateRecipes& value) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: ControlRoleRecipes derive_high_contrast_recipes(ControlRoleRecipes recipes, ControlVisualRole family)

   The forced high-contrast pass (T2 plan, E4; design language 5.8): ``recipes`` with its high_contrast and high_contrast_selected records derived from its ordinary ones. GUI.Forms' own high-contrast mode keeps each state's geometry and cues (corner radius, edge width, focus and default rings, offsets) and rebuilds the material as one solid plate inside one solid edge from a near-colourless palette by state, dropping gradients, images, keylines, per-edge borders and shadows. Selected parts take a white plate and black text inside their state's edge; a family whose text does not lie on a plate of its own (window, panel and card, whose children paint over the plate in their own states; choice, whose label lies on its parent's ground; editor, whose text lies on its well) never inverts: pressed keeps the plain plate inside the pressed edge, and selected the state's plate inside the selection line. Theme::create applies it to every family (``family``), so authored high-contrast records are replaced.

.. cpp:function:: ControlRoleRecipes derive_dark_recipes(ControlRoleRecipes recipes, ControlVisualRole family)

   The dark pass (stage T4 phase 5; design language 5.8 rule 3): ``recipes`` with its dark and dark_selected records derived from its ordinary and selected ones by the dark colour rule, for a family of role ``family``. Geometry, cues and structure are kept; every colour is mapped by what it paints (a face by its family: stock, well, chrome or accent; text, lines, cues, highlights and shades by their own rules), so the light stays upper left and inset stays inset.

.. cpp:function:: Color high_contrast_colour(ColourOrigin origin, ColourPosition position, ControlSurfaceState state, bool selected) noexcept

   The colour forced high contrast draws in place of one a theme declared (T3 EC4, owner decision D3 of 2026-10-28), for a part in ``state``, ``selected`` or not, from the same palette as derive_high_contrast_recipes: - a literal, by its position: a fill takes the state's plate, an edge its edge and text its text (a selected part's: a white plate and black text); a colour with no position takes the plate; - a role's colour, by its side: a foreground takes the text, or the edge in an edge position, and a background the plate; a single role of the relief ramp (and ``shadow``) takes the plate and a line the edge; except that the disabled roles' foregrounds take the disabled state's text or edge, ``invalid-ink-on-tint``'s foreground the invalid edge, ``focus`` and ``selection-line`` the hot edge, and the selection and on-accent pairs black on white, as a selected part. The forced high-contrast pass that draws with it is T3 P9's (EC6).

.. cpp:function:: inline std::vector<Color> provisional_accent_ramp()

   The accent ramp a theme offers parts that carry an AccentIndex (design language 8.5). Provisional: six translucent tints (amber, green, violet, orange, red, teal) laid over a part's material, until the default theme (stage T4) defines its own ramp and the engine (T2) its tint.

.. cpp:function:: inline std::vector<MaterialShadow> provisional_overlay_shadow()

   The overlay shadow (owner decision RB-37, design language 12.9; the E4 level of 3.4 scaled to small overlays): a soft shadow cast down and right, away from the one upper-left light, by surfaces that really overlap content (tooltips, overlays, menus). The ``shadow`` part role paints it around the casting surface. Two layers, in the units of MaterialShadow (blur is a CSS blur radius): a wide soft layer and a close contact layer, each offset right by at most half its drop (3.3). Provisional starting tokens until the default theme (stage T4) owns them.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t control_visual_role_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(ControlVisualRole::count)``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t control_surface_state_count

   Value: ``static_cast<std::size_t>(ControlSurfaceState::count)``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/types/cursor_image/cursor_image.hpp>``

.. cpp:type:: CursorImagesPtr = std::shared_ptr<const CursorImages>

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/types/damage_region/damage_region.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: DamageRegion damage_after_scroll_copy(const DamageRegion& damage, Rect clip, Point offset)

   The damage ``damage`` becomes once a scroll copy moved the pixels inside ``clip`` by ``offset`` (window coordinates): a rectangle's share inside the clip moves with its pixels and is cut to the clip; its share outside the clip stays where it was. The pixels the copy exposed are not included.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_marks/paint_marks.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_stroke_style(const StrokeStyle& style) noexcept

   Whether ``style`` can be drawn: a finite positive width, a finite miter limit of at least 1, at most maximum_dash_intervals dashes, an even dash count whose entries are finite, non-negative and not all zero, and a finite phase.

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_marker_style(const MarkerStyle& style) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: std::size_t marker_outline(MarkerShape shape, Point center, double size, std::array<Point, maximum_marker_vertices>& vertices) noexcept

   Fills ``vertices`` with the outline of a filled, non-circular marker of ``size`` centred on ``center``, clockwise in y-down coordinates, and returns how many it wrote; 0 for the circle and the stroked shapes, or when ``size`` is not finite and positive.

.. cpp:function:: std::size_t marker_lines(MarkerShape shape, Point center, double size, std::array<PaintSegment, maximum_marker_lines>& lines) noexcept

   Fills ``lines`` with the strokes of a stroked marker (cross, plus, asterisk) and returns how many it wrote; 0 for the filled shapes.

.. cpp:function:: bool valid_grid_pattern(const GridPattern& pattern) noexcept

.. cpp:function:: GridLevel select_grid_level(double world_spacing, double zoom, GridLevelBand band) noexcept

   Chooses the level for ``zoom`` (view units per world unit). ``world_spacing`` and ``zoom`` must be finite and positive and the band's maximum exactly 4 times its minimum; otherwise, or where no level within 64 steps of 0 fits, the result is level 0 at ``world_spacing * zoom`` with fade 1. Exact: a level's spacing is ``world_spacing * zoom`` times a power of 4.

.. cpp:function:: GridPattern grid_pattern_at_level(const GridLevel& level, Point origin, Color minor, Color major, double line_width) noexcept

   The pattern for a chosen level, majors every 4 minor lines, so a level's major lines are the next coarser level's minor lines. Minor lines fade in over the first half of the band (alpha scaled by 2 × fade) and turn toward ``major`` over the second (colour interpolated by 2 × fade − 1), so at fade 1 they look as the majors they become at the next level: nothing pops as the zoom crosses a level.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_dash_intervals

   The most on/off intervals a stroke's dash pattern holds. Value: ``8U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_marker_vertices

   The most vertices a filled marker's outline has (the diamond's and the square's four, the triangles' three). Value: ``4U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_marker_lines

   The most lines a stroked marker draws (the asterisk's three). Value: ``3U``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double minimum_grid_spacing

   The fewest units between two grid lines a painter draws: a finer grid is refused (draws nothing), which bounds a fill to its area divided by this squared. Value: ``2.0``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr double grid_level_ratio

   Value: ``4.0``.

.. cpp:namespace:: gui_forms

``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_recipes/paint_recipes.hpp>``

.. cpp:function:: Rect proportion_rect(Rect track, double proportion, FillDirection direction) noexcept

   The part of ``track`` a fill of ``proportion`` covers, growing from the edge ``direction`` names. The proportion is clamped to [0, 1]; NaN covers nothing (an empty rectangle at that edge).

.. cpp:function:: void paint_masked_fill(Painter& painter, Rect track, PaintCornerRadii track_radii, double proportion, FillDirection direction, Color fill)

   Piece C3: fills proportion_rect(track, proportion, direction) with ``fill``, clipped to the track's rounded shape (``track_radii``), so a short fill keeps the track's rounded end instead of poking out of it. Drawn as save, clip_rounded_rect_corners, fill_rect and restore; an empty portion draws nothing.

.. cpp:function:: double wcag_contrast_ratio(Color first, Color second) noexcept

   The WCAG 2 contrast ratio of two colours' sRGB channels, from 1 (equal) to 21 (black on white). Alpha is ignored: composite a translucent colour first.

.. cpp:function:: Color contrasting_text_color(Color background, Color light, Color dark) noexcept

   The contrast rule for text over a fill: of ``light`` and ``dark``, the one with the greater contrast ratio against ``background`` (dark on a tie).

.. cpp:function:: void paint_text_over_fill(Painter& painter, Rect track, Rect fill, Point origin, std::string_view text, FontSpec font, Color text_color, Color on_fill_color)

   Piece C4: one run of text drawn twice under complementary clips, in ``on_fill_color`` where it lies over ``fill`` and in ``text_color`` over the rest of ``track`` (a value bar's or progress bar's centred label). The fill part is ``fill`` within ``track``; the rest is at most four bands of ``track`` around it, which share the fill part's edges exactly, so every pixel of the run is drawn once. ``origin`` is the baseline start, as draw_text_utf8 takes it; text outside ``track`` is clipped. Usually ``on_fill_color = contrasting_text_color(fill colour, light, dark)``.

.. cpp:function:: JoinedSegmentPosition joined_segment_position(std::size_t index, std::size_t count) noexcept

   The position of segment ``index`` of ``count`` (an index past the end is ``last``; a count of 0 or 1 is ``only``).

.. cpp:function:: PaintCornerRadii joined_segment_radii(JoinedSegmentPosition position, JoinAxis axis, double radius) noexcept

   The corner radii a segment at ``position`` takes: ``radius`` on its outer corners and 0 on the corners it shares with a neighbour.

.. cpp:function:: void paint_joined_segments(Painter& painter, std::span<const Rect> segments, std::span<const Color> faces, JoinAxis axis, const JoinedSegmentsStyle& style)

   Draws a joined row or column: each segment's face in its colour (``faces`` holds one per segment) with joined_segment_radii, then one seam centred in the gap between each pair of neighbours, then the outer edge round the segments' union. ``segments`` lie in order along ``axis``; a count mismatch, no segments, or a non-finite or negative radius or width draws nothing.

   Value: ``32U``.

``#include <gui_forms/types/paint_types/paint_types.hpp>``

.. cpp:var:: constexpr TypeTag<CursorKind> cursor_kind

``#include <gui_forms/types/painter/painter.hpp>``

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_paint_group_depth

   The deepest groups nest (T2, coordinator decision Q2/Q3). A group begun deeper is refused by name: it draws without isolation (as clip_rect(bounds) under a save), and the refusal is counted. Value: ``4``.

.. cpp:var:: constexpr std::size_t maximum_checker_cells
